<a id="q2058"></a>

## Q2058. Force dominating models at optimal density

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

Does an absolute C>0 exist such that, for every integer t≥2, every graph of average degree at least Ct log t contains a dominating K_t-model?

**Context.** Origin for question 2058: Density conjecture: Illingworth–Wood, restated here. Setup for question 2058: A dominating K_t-model in a finite simple graph is an ordered sequence of disjoint nonempty connected vertex sets X₁,…,X_t such that every vertex of X_j has a neighbour in X_i whenever i&lt;j.

**Source.** António Girão; Sergey Norin; Youri Tamitegama; Jane Tan. *Two Relaxations of the Dominating Hadwiger’s Conjecture*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12126) Location: §5, first paragraph, p. 18.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2058, checked 6 October 2026: the proper-colouring disproof does not settle this density bound; no resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35361)


<a id="q2059"></a>

## Q2059. Find high-girth high-dichromatic subdigraphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3) · **Collection** 21

For all integers k≥0 and ℓ≥3, is there f(k,ℓ) such that every tournament of dichromatic number at least f(k,ℓ) contains a subdigraph of dichromatic number greater than k and girth greater than ℓ?

**Context.** Origin for question 2059: Charbit–Coulomb’s directed strengthening of the Erdős–Hajnal large-girth conjecture. Setup for question 2059: The dichromatic number of a finite digraph is the fewest vertex classes inducing acyclic digraphs. Its girth is the girth of its underlying undirected graph; a subdigraph need not be induced.

**Source.** Pierre Charbit; Samuel Coulomb. *Digraphs of Large Girth and Dichromatic Number in Tournaments with Large Dichromatic Number*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21895) Location: Conjecture 3, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2059, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found.


<a id="q2060"></a>

## Q2060. Order edges to distinguish neighbours

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 10) · **Collection** 21

For every finite simple connected graph G other than K₂ or an even cycle, and every proper edge colouring w, is there a total edge order making the resulting sequences different at the ends of every edge?

**Context.** Origin for question 2060: Gorzkowska–Kwaśny, reversing the fixed-order sequence-colouring problem of Seamone–Stevens. Setup for question 2060: Given a proper edge colouring w and one total order of E(G), each vertex receives the sequence of colours on its incident edges in that order.

**Source.** Aleksandra Gorzkowska; Jakub Kwaśny. *Distinguishing adjacent vertices by ordering edges*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11832) Location: Conjecture 10, p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2060, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found.


<a id="q2061"></a>

## Q2061. Force non-nested monochromatic matchings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

For every k≥1, does every red/blue edge-colouring of the ordered complete graph on 3k−1 vertices contain a monochromatic k-edge matching with no nested pair?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2061, 2062: Ramsey conjecture: Barát–Gyárfás–Tóth (2024). Strong Turán conjecture: Barát–Freschi–Tóth. Setup for questions 2061, 2062: Vertices have a linear order. Disjoint edges ab,cd (a&lt;b,c&lt;d) are nested if a&lt;c&lt;d&lt;b or c&lt;a&lt;b&lt;d; crossing if a&lt;c&lt;b&lt;d or c&lt;a&lt;d&lt;b; otherwise separated. A strongly non-nested matching partitions into blocks, crossing within blocks and separated between blocks.

**Source.** János Barát; Andrea Freschi; Géza Tóth. *Matchings avoiding ordered patterns*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15461) Location: §3.2, p. 14.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2061, 2062, checked 6 October 2026: both exact bounds remain unresolved.


<a id="q2062"></a>

## Q2062. Determine the strong non-nested Turán number

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.6) · **Collection** 21

For all integers k≥1 and n≥2k, is (k−1)n the largest number of edges in an ordered n-vertex graph containing no strongly non-nested k-edge matching?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2061, 2062: Ramsey conjecture: Barát–Gyárfás–Tóth (2024). Strong Turán conjecture: Barát–Freschi–Tóth. Setup for questions 2061, 2062: Vertices have a linear order. Disjoint edges ab,cd (a&lt;b,c&lt;d) are nested if a&lt;c&lt;d&lt;b or c&lt;a&lt;b&lt;d; crossing if a&lt;c&lt;b&lt;d or c&lt;a&lt;d&lt;b; otherwise separated. A strongly non-nested matching partitions into blocks, crossing within blocks and separated between blocks.

**Source.** János Barát; Andrea Freschi; Géza Tóth. *Matchings avoiding ordered patterns*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15461) Location: Conjecture 2.6, p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2061, 2062, checked 6 October 2026: both exact bounds remain unresolved.


<a id="q2063"></a>

## Q2063. Four-colour cycles with quartet constraints

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.1) · **Collection** 21

If G and H have the same vertex set, G is a disjoint union of cycles, and H is a disjoint union of copies of K₄, must G∪H be 4-colourable?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2063, 2064: Problem 3.1: Haxell. Problem 5.1: Lužar–Soták; bipartite predecessor in Janicová–Madaras–Soták–Lužar (2017). Setup for questions 2063, 2064: Graphs are finite and simple. A 2-homogeneous colouring is a proper vertex colouring with exactly two distinct colours in every vertex’s open neighbourhood.

**Source.** Alfréd Onderko (editor), with named proposers below. *Open problems of the 33rd Workshop on Cycles and Colourings*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02892) Location: Problem 3.1, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2063, 2064, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23462)

Does every cubic graph admitting a 2-homogeneous colouring admit one using at most four colours?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2063, 2064: Problem 3.1: Haxell. Problem 5.1: Lužar–Soták; bipartite predecessor in Janicová–Madaras–Soták–Lužar (2017). <a id="q2065"></a>

## Q2065. Determine the rainbow-path Ramsey threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2) · **Collection** 21

For every disconnected finite graph H with chromatic number at least three, is f(H,P₅)=R₃(H)?

**Context.** Origin for question 2065: Li–Besse–Magnant–Wang–Watts (2020), restated by Li–Liu. Setup for question 2065: R₃(H) is the three-colour Ramsey number of H. Let f(H,P₅) be the least N such that every edge-colouring of K_N, with any number of colours, contains a monochromatic H or a rainbow five-vertex path. Copies need not be induced.

**Source.** Xihe Li; Xiangxiang Liu. *Constrained Ramsey numbers for rainbow P₅*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18243) Location: Conjecture 1.2 and Theorem 1.3, pp. 2–3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2065, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found.


<a id="q2066"></a>

## Q2066. Count fixed occurrences of 4312

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.1) · **Collection** 21

For every fixed integer r≥1, does some K_r>0 satisfy S_(n,r)(4312)∼K_r S_(n,0)(4312) as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for question 2066: Conway–Guttmann (2023), restated by Waite. Setup for question 2066: Let S_(n,r)(4312) count permutations of [n] with exactly r subsequences of four positions whose entries have relative order 4312; S_(n,0) counts avoiders.

**Source.** Michael Waite. *Permutations with a fixed number of occurrences of a pattern: A case generalizing 231*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21625) Location: Conjecture 6.1, unresolved case in §6, p. 10.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2066, checked 6 October 2026: no later resolution found.


<a id="q2152"></a>

## Q2152. Characterize fundamental power-sum systems

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.5.1) · **Collection** 22

For integers 1≤c₁<⋯&lt;c_n, does K(p_(c₁),…,p_(c_n))=K(x₁,…,x_n)^(S_n) hold exactly when N₀∖{c₁,…,c_n} is a numerical semigroup?

**Context.** Origin for question 2152: Kakeya (1925, 1927), restated by Wang. Setup for question 2152: Let K have characteristic zero, x₁,…,x_n be indeterminates, n≥1, and p_j=Σ\_(i=1)^n x_i^j. A numerical semigroup is a cofinite additive submonoid of N₀ containing 0.

**Source.** Chengyang Wang. *Semigroups of Polyhedral Lattice Points: Convexity, Combinatorics, and Algebra*. 2024. Advisor(s): Jesús A. De Loera. [primary source](https://www.math.ucdavis.edu/~webfiles/dissertations/202403_Chengyang_Wang_Dissertation.pdf) Location: Conjecture 1.5.1, p. 22; Definition 1.1.9, p. 10.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2152, checked 6 October 2026: source leaves this open; later searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/9104r5x6) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.01175) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jsc.2025.102542)

## Q2153. Rainbow-connect under an Ore bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.3) · **Collection** 22

Does every n-vertex graph satisfying deg(u)+deg(v)≥n for each nonadjacent pair u,v admit an rc2-colouring?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2153, 2154: Barát–Boyadzhiyska–Freschi; Ore version strengthens earlier rainbow-connectivity questions. Setup for questions 2153, 2154: Graphs are finite and simple; an rc2-colouring colours edges with two colours so every vertex pair has a path with distinct edge colours.

**Source.** János Barát; Simona Boyadzhiyska; Andrea Freschi. *Rainbow connecting 2-colorings of super-Dirac graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11437) Location: Question 6.3, p. 11.

<a id="q2155"></a>

## Q2155. Determine proper book hat numbers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.3) · **Collection** 22

For each fixed integer k≥3, determine max_(n≥1) HG_P(B_(k,n)).

**Context.** Origin for questions 2155, 2156: These authors’ proper-colouring variant of hat guessing. Setup for questions 2155, 2156: For a finite simple graph G, HG_P(G) is the largest q admitting deterministic functions f_v:[q]^(N(v))→[q] such that every proper [q]-colouring has some v whose own colour equals f_v of its neighbours’ colours. Guesses are simultaneous. B_(k,n)=K_k joined to an independent n-set.

**Source.** Sam Adriaensen; Peter Bentley; Anurag Bishnoi; Wouter Cames van Batenburg; Michael Kreiger; Lars van der Kuil; Saptarshi Mandal; Anurag Ramachandran; James Tuite. *Hat guessing with proper colorings*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04909) Location: Problem 7.3, p. 10; Zhai (2026), §9.2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2155, 2156, checked 6 October 2026: Zhai solves k=2 books, retaining k≥3. K_n−e has small-case proof claims, but no general evaluation located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.04909v4) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25274) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.25274v3) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04124) · [5](https://vibemathed.com/problem/the-proper-hat-guessing-number-of-k-8-e)

<a id="q2157"></a>

## Q2157. Force single-colour triangle tilings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.6) · **Collection** 22

For n≥25 and δ≥4n/5, must G contain m vertex-disjoint triangles all of one colour, where m=floor((δ+1)/5) if δ≥15n/17, m=floor((4δ−3n+1)/3) if 6n/7≤δ≤15n/17, and m=ceil((5δ−4n)/2) if 4n/5≤δ≤6n/7?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2157, 2158: Balogh–Freschi–Treglown’s dense extensions of Burr–Erdős–Spencer and Moon. Setup for questions 2157, 2158: Graphs are finite, simple and red/blue edge-coloured; n=|V(G)| and δ=δ(G). A triangle tiling consists of vertex-disjoint triangles.

**Source.** József Balogh; Andrea Freschi; Andrew Treglown. *Ramsey-type problems for tilings in dense graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejc.2025.104228) Location: Question 1.6, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2157, 2158, checked 6 October 2026: journal questions remain unresolved in later searches.

**Further links.** [1](https://real.mtak.hu/232488/1/1-s2.0-S0195669825001179-main.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13876)

## Q2158. Remove the triangle-tiling error

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 22

For all sufficiently large n, if 5n/6≤δ≤7n/8, must G contain a tiling of at least floor((4δ−3n)/2) individually monochromatic triangles, allowing different triangles to have different colours?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2157, 2158: Balogh–Freschi–Treglown’s dense extensions of Burr–Erdős–Spencer and Moon. Setup for questions 2157, 2158: Graphs are finite, simple and red/blue edge-coloured; n=|V(G)| and δ=δ(G). A triangle tiling consists of vertex-disjoint triangles.

**Source.** József Balogh; Andrea Freschi; Andrew Treglown. *Ramsey-type problems for tilings in dense graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejc.2025.104228) Location: §5.2, p. 19; Theorem 1.7(M.2), p. 4.

<a id="q2159"></a>

## Q2159. Determine planar stable-colouring growth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 22

Determine the asymptotic order of max{χ\_st(G): G is planar and |V(G)|=n} as n→∞.

**Context.** Origin for question 2159: Koana–Oh–Yoneda (2026). Setup for question 2159: At each vertex of finite simple G, fix a strict ranking of [k]. A proper [k]-colouring is stable if the digraph having arc u→v for adjacent u,v whenever u prefers v’s colour to its own is acyclic. Let χ\_st(G) be the least k for which every ranking profile admits a stable colouring.

**Source.** Tomohiro Koana; Yeeseok Oh; Hirotaka Yoneda. *Graph Coloring with Color Preferences*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00569) Location: §5, p. 14; §3.7, pp. 10–11.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2159, checked 6 October 2026: source leaves this open; later searches found no resolution.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B7: submitted unresolved): No new analysis or status promotion in this return. Remaining/limits: not_attempted Intake: Scope and identity checked; no independent proof or renewed literature audit of this question. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.00569v1)


<a id="q2160"></a>

## Q2160. Optimize defective blowup distortion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4) · **Collection** 22

Determine sup_(G,d≥0) χ(G)/χ^d(G⊠K_(d+1)), where d is an integer.

**Context.** Origin for question 2160: Norin–Steiner’s quantitative replacement for the refuted Guo–Kang–Zwaneveld equality. Setup for question 2160: For finite nonempty simple G, χ^d(G) is the fewest vertex colours with monochromatic maximum degree≤d. G⊠K_(d+1) replaces vertices by (d+1)-cliques and edges by complete joins.

**Source.** Sergey Norin; Raphael Steiner. *Defective coloring of blowups*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14039) Location: Problem 4, p. 3 (preprint Problem 1.4).

**Literature check.** Status for question 2160, checked 6 October 2026: source leaves this open; later searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01548) · [2](https://www.combinatorics.org/ojs/index.php/eljc/article/download/v33i1p17/pdf/)


<a id="q2161"></a>

## Q2161. Guarantee asymptotically half-degree

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4) · **Collection** 22

For every ε>0, is there d₀ such that δ\_B(G)≥(1/2−ε)d for every integer d≥d₀ and every finite simple d-regular graph G?

**Context.** Origin for question 2161: Asymptotic question: Beck (2008), Problem 16.1; cubic classification: Győrffy. Setup for question 2161: On a finite simple graph, Maker moves first and players alternately claim one unclaimed edge until none remain. δ\_B(G) is the greatest minimum degree Breaker can guarantee in the spanning graph of Breaker’s edges. Both players see all moves.

**Source.** Lajos Győrffy. *Degree Game for Special Regular Graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11007) Location: Open Problem 4, second question, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2161, checked 6 October 2026: August follow-on improves the universal bound to d/3−o(d), leaving these targets open.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.11007v1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20644) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.20644v1)

## Q2162. Bound simple critical average degree

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 22

Must every Δ-critical simple graph on n vertices satisfy d̄≥Δ−1+3/n?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2162, 2163: Conjecture 1: Vizing (1968); 2: these authors; 3: Goldberg (1984). Setup for questions 2162, 2163: Graphs are finite loopless multigraphs on at least two vertices. Write Δ for maximum degree, d̄=2|E|/|V|, χ′ for chromatic index. G is Δ-critical if χ′(G)=Δ+1 and every proper subgraph is Δ-edge-colourable.

**Source.** Guantao Chen; Yuying Ma; Yimo Su; Shengze Wang. *Average degrees of edge-Δ-critical multigraphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12271) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2162, 2163, checked 6 October 2026: three conjectures retained in v1; no later resolution located.

<a id="q2164"></a>

## Q2164. Bound colours without balanced thetas

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 10.2) · **Collection** 22

For every ℓ≥2 and t≥2, does a function f_(ℓ,t):N→N exist such that χ(G)≤f_(ℓ,t)(ω(G)) whenever G has no induced balanced t-theta whose paths have length at least ℓ?

**Context.** Origin for question 2164: Girão–Hunter, Conjecture 10.2. Setup for question 2164: Graphs are finite and simple. A balanced t-theta is the union of t internally vertex-disjoint paths, all having the same two distinct endpoints and the same length; it has no other edges. χ and ω denote chromatic and clique numbers.

**Source.** António Girão; Zach Hunter. *Induced Subdivisions in K_(s,s)-Free Graphs With Polynomial Average Degree*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1093/imrn/rnaf025) Location: Conjecture 10.2, p. 22.

## Q2165. Polynomially colour every forest-free class

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2) · **Collection** 22

For every finite forest T, does a polynomial p_T exist such that χ(G)≤p_T(ω(G)) for every T-free graph G?

**Context.** Origin for question 2165: Polynomial Gyárfás–Sumner conjecture; path version: Esperet–Lemoine–Maffray–Morel (2013). Setup for question 2165: Graphs are finite and simple; χ(G) and ω(G) denote chromatic and clique numbers. T-free means no induced subgraph isomorphic to T.

**Source.** Tung H. Nguyen. *Polynomial χ-boundedness for excluding P₅*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24907) Location: Conjecture 1.2, p. 2.

<a id="q2166"></a>

## Q2166. Strongly colour bipartite edges

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 22

Must every such G satisfy χ′\_s(G)≤Δ\_AΔ\_B?

**Context.** Origin for question 2166: Brualdi–Quinn Massey (1993). Setup for question 2166: For finite simple bipartite G with nonempty parts A,B, let Δ\_A and Δ\_B be their maximum vertex degrees. A strong edge-colouring partitions E(G) into induced matchings; χ′\_s(G) is its minimum colour count.

**Source.** Yanli Hao; Tianchi Yang; Xingxing Yu. *Strong chromatic index of bipartite graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23824) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2166, checked 6 October 2026: latest v2 and July follow-on retain the unrestricted conjecture.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.23824v2) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17421)


<a id="q2167"></a>

## Q2167. Strongly colour all edges

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 22

Must every G satisfy χ(L(G)²)≤5Δ(G)²/4?

**Context.** Origin for question 2167: Erdős–Nešetřil (1980s). Setup for question 2167: Graphs are finite and simple. L(G) is the line graph; its square joins distinct vertices at distance at most two.

**Source.** Hitesh Kumar; Bojan Mohar; Shivaramakrishna Pragada. *An improved bound for the strong clique index of graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02698) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 1.

## Q2219. Polytopal realization of convex codes

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1.11) · **Collection** 23

Let C⊆2^[n] contain ∅ and admit open convex U_i⊆R^d with C={σ⊆[n]:(∩\_{i∈σ}U_i)∖(∪\_{j∉σ}U_j)≠∅}, using ∩∅=R^d. Must the same code be realizable by possibly empty interiors of bounded convex polytopes in some finite-dimensional Euclidean space?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2219, 2220: Polytope conjecture: Kunin–Lienkaemper–Rosen 2020; isolated-subset question: Trang.

**Source.** Trong-Thuc Trang. *Algebraic and Topological Methods in Computational Neuroscience*. 2026. Advisor(s): Zvi Rosen, Timothy J. Ford. [primary source](https://digitalcommons.fau.edu/etd_general/433/) Location: §1.1.3, Conjecture 1.1.11, p. 8.

Determine the asymptotic growth of I(n)=max_C |{I⊆C:I is isolated}|, over intersection-closed C⊆2^[n] containing ∅. A nonempty intersection-closed I is isolated if, for μ=∩I, no σ∈C∖I contains any τ∈I∖{μ}.

**Context.** Origin for questions 2219, 2220: Polytope conjecture: Kunin–Lienkaemper–Rosen 2020; isolated-subset question: Trang.

<a id="q2251"></a>

## Q2251. Remove asymmetric tree logarithms

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 23

Do c>0 and N₀ exist such that, whenever n≥ν≥N₀, if s₂≥t₂, ν≥t₁, Δ(T)≤cn and Δ(S)≤cν, then R(T,S)=max{n+s₂,ν+min{t₂,ν},min{2t₁,2ν},2s₁}−1?

**Context.** Origin for question 2251: Yan, extending the symmetric tree result with Montgomery and Pavez-Signé. Setup for question 2251: R(T,S) is the least N forcing a red T or blue S in every red/blue edge-colouring of K_N. Trees T,S have n≥ν vertices and bipartition sizes t₁≥t₂, s₁≥s₂, respectively.

**Source.** Jun Yan. *Asymmetric Ramsey numbers of trees*. 2025. Advisor(s): Richard Montgomery. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.15673) Location: Question 6.1 and Theorem 1.3; pp. 3,28.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2251, checked 6 October 2026: source leaves these targets open; later searches found no resolution.

## Q2252. Minimize ordered path Ramsey numbers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 29) · **Collection** 23

For every n≥2 and every ordered n-vertex path P, must R_<(AP_n)≤R_<(P)?

**Context.** Origin for question 2252: Balko–Cibulka–Král–Kynčl, Problem 29. Setup for question 2252: Graphs are finite and simple, with a total vertex order; copies preserve it. R_<(H) is the least N forcing a monochromatic H in every red/blue colouring of K_N. AP_n traverses 1,n,2,n−1,….

**Source.** Gaurav Kucheriya; Allan Lo; Jan Petr; Amedeo Sgueglia; Jun Yan. *Ordered Ramsey and Turán numbers of alternating paths and their variants*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12358) Location: §1.1, p. 3; citing Problem 29.

<a id="q2253"></a>

## Q2253. Classify perfect-tree EKR parameters

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.7) · **Collection** 23

For which integers r≥2, h≥1 and k≥1 is the perfect r-ary tree with h levels k-EKR?

**Context.** Origin for question 2253: Iľkovič–Yan, motivated by Holroyd–Talbot. Setup for question 2253: A perfect r-ary tree with h levels has r children at each nonleaf and all leaves at distance h−1 from the root. A graph is k-EKR if every pairwise-intersecting family of independent k-vertex sets has size at most the largest family of independent k-sets containing one fixed vertex.

**Source.** Daniel Iľkovič; Jun Yan. *Distribution of independent sets in perfect r-ary trees*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16953) Location: Question 1.7, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2253, checked 6 October 2026: source leaves these targets open; later searches found no resolution.


<a id="q2254"></a>

## Q2254. Complete bipartite rainbow matchings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 23

For every n≥1, must n matchings of size n+1 in a bipartite graph admit a rainbow matching of size n?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2254, 2255, 2256: Bipartite: Aharoni–Berger; general graphs: Aharoni et al., explicitly Gao–Ramadurai–Wanless–Wormald; hypergraph asymptotics: Bowtell et al. Setup for questions 2254, 2255, 2256: Collections of matchings may overlap or repeat. A rainbow matching chooses vertex-disjoint edges with distinct assigned collection indices, each edge belonging to its assigned matching. Hosts are finite and simple; r-uniform edges have r vertices.

**Source.** Candida Bowtell; Andrea Freschi; Gal Kronenberg; Jun Yan. *A note on improved bounds for hypergraph rainbow matching problems*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.03216) Location: Conjecture 1.3, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2254, 2255, 2256, checked 6 October 2026: August Delcourt–Postle reaffirms both exact graph bounds; no hypergraph-asymptotic resolution located.

<a id="q2257"></a>

## Q2257. Classify minimal-density tent families

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.2) · **Collection** 23

For each r≥2, which subfamilies F⊆{D_i:1≤i≤⌊r/2⌋} satisfy π(F)=r!/r^r?

**Context.** Origin for question 2257: Iľkovič–Yan, extending Chao–Yu’s tent theorem. Setup for question 2257: For r≥2 and 1≤i≤⌊r/2⌋, D_i is the r-uniform hypergraph with three edges e₀,e₁,e₂ satisfying e₀=(e₀∩e₁)⊔(e₀∩e₂), |e₀∩e₁|=i, and e₁∩e₂={v} with v∉e₀; all other vertices are distinct. For a forbidden family F, π(F)=lim_(n→∞)ex(n,F)/binom(n,r), with ordinary subgraph avoidance.

**Source.** Daniel Iľkovič; Jun Yan. *An improved hypergraph Mantel’s Theorem*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14474) Location: Question 5.2, p. 15; tent definition, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2257, checked 6 October 2026: Ma–Zhu improves the initial-segment bound; arbitrary tent-subfamily classification remains unresolved in inspected sources.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11373) · [2](https://doi.org/10.4208/aam.OA-2025-0016)


<a id="q2258"></a>

## Q2258. Balance degree counts within two

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 23

Must every d-regular n-vertex graph G have a spanning subgraph H with |m_i(H)−n/(d+1)|≤2 for every integer 0≤i≤d?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2258, 2259: Degree counts: Alon–Wei (2023); irregularity strength: Faudree–Lehel (1987). Setup for questions 2258, 2259: Graphs are finite and simple. Write m_i(H)=|{v:deg_H(v)=i}|. The irregularity strength s(G) is the least k admitting edge weights in {1,…,k} whose incident-weight sums are different at every pair of vertices.

**Source.** Richard Montgomery; Alexey Pokrovskiy; Benny Sudakov. *Nearly-uniform degree distributions in spanning subgraphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30612) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2258, 2259, checked 6 October 2026: asymptotic degree balancing is solved, but these exact uniform bounds remain open.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06465) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.13095)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1) · **Collection** 23

For integers n≥1 and k≥2, determine the least m such that every word with m repeats contains 0^(k+2), (0011⋯nn)^e for e∈{id,rev}, or (012⋯n)^(e₁)(012⋯n)^(e₂) for e₁,e₂∈{id,rev}.

**Context.** Origin for question 2264: Celano–Ollson–Velankar–Yan. Setup for question 2264: Words are finite over N. A repeat is an entry whose value appeared earlier. ## Q2351. Sharpen skeletal Ramsey towers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.1) · **Collection** 24

For each q≥2, is there C_q>0 such that every n-vertex 3-graph H with d_1(H)=d≥1 satisfies r(H;q)≤2^(2^(C_q d))n?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2351, 2352: Fox–Sankar–Simkin–Tidor–Zhou. Setup for questions 2351, 2352: For k-uniform H, d_i(H) is the degeneracy of its (i+1)-uniform shadow: the maximum minimum degree over its nonempty subhypergraphs. Put d_max=max_(1≤i&lt;k)d_i. r(H;q) is the ordinary q-colour Ramsey number; ex(N,H) maximizes edges of H-free k-graphs.

**Source.** Jacob Fox; Maya Sankar; Michael Simkin; Jonathan Tidor; Yunkun Zhou. *Ramsey and Turán numbers of sparse hypergraphs*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.00359) Location: Conjecture 6.1, p. 30.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3) · **Collection** 24

Does every properly edge-coloured loopless digraph with all in-degrees and out-degrees equal to d≥1 contain a directed rainbow path of d−1 edges?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2353, 2354, 2355: Directed path: Bucić et al.; tree slack: these authors; cube average degree: Crawford–King–Spiro. Setup for questions 2353, 2354, 2355: Graphs are finite and simple; antiparallel arcs are allowed. Rainbow means distinct edge colours. Proper digraph colouring forbids equal colours at a common tail or common head. Q_n joins binary n-vectors differing in one coordinate.

**Source.** Nicholas Crawford; Maya Sankar; Carl Schildkraut; Sam Spiro. *Rainbow Trees in Hypercubes*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14186) Location: Question 1.3, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2353, 2354, 2355, checked 6 October 2026: September directed-path bound is asymptotic; these exact targets remain unresolved in checked sources.

## Q2357. Improve universal two-complex exponent

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2) · **Collection** 24

## Q2365. Sharpen path parity covers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 24

Does every finite simple graph G satisfy p₂(G)≤max{o(G)/2,⌈Δ(G)/2⌉}+1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2365, 2366: Borgwardt and coauthors. Setup for questions 2365, 2366: For finite simple G, p₂(G) (respectively c₂(G)) is the minimum number of simple paths (cycles) in the complete graph on V(G) whose edge-set symmetric difference is E(G). No extra vertices are allowed. Let o(G) count odd-degree vertices; Eulerian here means all degrees even, without requiring connectedness.

**Source.** Steffen Borgwardt; Calum Buchanan; Eric Culver; Bryce Frederickson; Puck Rombach; Youngho Yoo. *Path odd-covers of graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.disc.2026.115078) Location: Problem 1, final journal §5.

## Q2460. Locate the connectivity-coverage equality

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.3) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Given n≥2 and r≥1, determine the smallest s∈{1,…,r} for which f(n,r,s)=g(n,r,s).

**Context.** Origin for questions 2460, 2461: Alon–Bucić–Christoph–Krivelevich. Setup for questions 2460, 2461: For edge-colourings χ:E(K_n)→[r], let G_χ(S) retain colours S. For n≥2 and 1≤s≤r, f(n,r,s)=min_χ max_(|S|≤s) largest-component size of G_χ(S); g(n,r,s)=min_χ max_(|S|≤s) number of nonisolated vertices of G_χ(S). Hypergraphs are finite and simple.

**Source.** Noga Alon; Matija Bucić; Micha Christoph; Michael Krivelevich. *The power of many colours*. 2024. [primary source](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/forum-of-mathematics-sigma/article/power-of-many-colours/DE9A531B5AFE6CB470AADC002CAEB553) Location: Question 4.3, §4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2460, 2461, checked 6 October 2026: August spanning-threshold estimates do not settle these exact questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.15387) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06418) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2024.120)

<a id="q2462"></a>

## Q2462. Evaluate nondegenerate limiting densities

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.1) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

If no nonempty subset of the coefficients sums to zero, determine lim_(ε↓0)d(L,ε).

**Context.** Origin for question 2462: Bucić–Christoph–Kim–Lee–Sivashankar. Setup for question 2462: Let L:Σ\_(i=1)^k c_i x_i=0, k≥3, c_i∈Z∖{0}. A⊆F_p∖{0} is L-free if it has no solution with pairwise distinct coordinates. Cay_p(A) joins distinct u,v when v−u∈A or u−v∈A. Define d(L,ε)=limsup_(p→∞, p prime) p⁻¹ max{|A|:A is L-free, α(Cay_p(A))≤εp}.

**Source.** Matija Bucić; Micha Christoph; Jaehoon Kim; Hyunwoo Lee; Varun Sivashankar. *On a Ramsey–Turán variant of Roth’s theorem*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1112/blms.70436) Location: Problem 5.1, final journal §5 (preprint Problem 5.2).

**Literature check.** Status for question 2462, checked 6 October 2026: final journal retains this limit problem; its linear-decay conjecture is refuted.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: For every fixed k>=3 and nonzero integer c, the equation c(x1+...+xk)=0 satisfies d(L,epsilon)=1/k for every fixed epsilon>0. The general epsilon-down-to-zero limit exists by monotonicity; its value for arbitrary nondegenerate coefficients remains unresolved. Restricted scope: All coefficients equal one fixed nonzero integer c for the evaluated density. Remaining: Arbitrary nondegenerate integer coefficient tuples are not evaluated. Recorded from the external B2 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected exact checks in the intake; verification archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/B2_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B2-R2: auxiliary proof submission): For every fixed k>=3, c!=0, and epsilon>0, d(c(x1+...+xk)=0,epsilon)=1/k. Location: Part A, section 2. Restricted scope: Equal coefficients. Full retained question(s) remain unresolved; consult intake checks and archive limits. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22831) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05490)


<a id="q2463"></a>

## Q2463. Attain periodic word concentration

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.5) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For every w and d≥1, is C_d(w) attained by some finite grid Γ?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2463, 2464, 2465: Halberstam–Schildkraut, following Patchell–Spiro and Alon–Kravitz. Setup for questions 2463, 2464, 2465: For a word w with ≥2 distinct letters, grids are maps Γ:G→Σ, G=∏\_(i=1)^d Z/n_iZ, n_i≥1. Count pairs (p,v)∈G×({−1,0,1}^d∖{0}) satisfying Γ(p+jv)=w_j for all 0≤j<|w|. Let c_d(w,Γ) be this count divided by |G|, and C_d(w)=sup_Γ c_d(w,Γ).

**Source.** Zachary Halberstam; Carl Schildkraut. *Words with Repeated Letters in a Grid*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19678) Location: Question 2.5, p. 6.

## Q2466. Compare directed and undirected circumference

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.2) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Let c(n),d(n) be the minimum circumferences of connected vertex-transitive n-vertex graphs and digraphs, respectively. Is d(n)=Θ(c(n)) as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2466, 2467: Bucić–Hendrey–Mohar–Steiner–Yepremyan. Setup for questions 2466, 2467: Digraphs are finite, loopless and simple, with opposite arcs allowed. Vertex-transitive means the automorphism group acts transitively on vertices; connected means the underlying graph is connected. Circumference is the largest number of vertices on a directed cycle (ordinary cycle for graphs).

**Source.** Matija Bucić; Kevin Hendrey; Bojan Mohar; Raphael Steiner; Liana Yepremyan. *Long cycles in vertex transitive digraphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16333) Location: Conjecture 4.2, p. 12.

## Q2468. Determine the Schur chromatic threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.1) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

What is the exact value of δχ?

**Context.** Origin for question 2468: Liu–Wu–Yang–Zhang. Setup for question 2468: For prime p, A⊆F_p is Schur-free if no pairwise distinct x,y,z∈A satisfy x+y=z. Cay_p(A) joins distinct u,v when ±(u−v)∈A. Define δχ=inf{d>0:∃C_d<∞, ∀ primes p, ∀ Schur-free A⊆F_p, |A|≥dp⇒χ(Cay_p(A))≤C_d}.

**Source.** Hong Liu; Zhuo Wu; Ningyuan Yang; Shengtong Zhang. *Chromatic thresholds for linear equations and recurrence*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05490) Location: Problem 6.1, p. 27.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2468, checked 6 October 2026: source retains these questions; bounded later searches found no resolution.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B2: submitted unresolved): Primary-source qualitative threshold classification does not give the exact Schur threshold.; Small Cayley independence number and unbounded Cayley chromatic number are distinct constraints; fixed integer scalar multiplication alone does not introduce a second independent group law. Remaining/limits: No new exact value or quantitative bound for the retained Schur chromatic threshold is established. [Report](../../solutions/b2-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://www.ibs.re.kr/ecopro/papers/)

## Q2554. Treewidth-two square-root blowups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

For every fixed integer h≥1, does a constant C_h exist such that every n-vertex K_h-minor-free graph embeds as a subgraph of H⊠K_{⌈C_h√n⌉} for some H with treewidth at most 2?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2554, 2555: Monash PhD; supervisors David Wood and Norm Do. Origin for questions 2554, 2555: Blowups: Distel (2025–2026). Coarse planarity: Georgakopoulos–Papasoglu. Setup for questions 2554, 2555: Graphs are finite, simple, undirected and unweighted. A q-fat minor model has mutually q-separated branch sets, mutually q-separated branch paths, and distance ≥q between nonincident branch sets and paths. A c-quasi-isometry φ satisfies d_G/c−1≤d_H∘(φ,φ)≤c d_G+c and every target vertex is within c of its image. H⊠K_b replaces vertices by b-cliques and edges by complete bipartite graphs.

**Source.** Marc Alexander Robert Distel. *Global Structure of Minor-Free Graphs and Beyond*. Monash University, 2026. Advisor(s): David Wood; Norm Do. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.26180/32329152.v1) Location: Final paragraph of Chapter 7, p. 181 (PDF p. 192).

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2556, 2557: Charles University PhD; advisor Mykhaylo Tyomkyn. Origin for questions 2556, 2557: Spiders: Hons et al. (2025). Paths: Kucheriya–Tardos. Setup for questions 2556, 2557: Digraphs are finite, loopless, without parallel arcs; oriented graphs additionally exclude opposite arcs. S^-\_{k,ℓ} consists of ℓ internally disjoint directed k-edge paths ending at one common center. For an edge-ordered graph F, ex_<(n,F) is the maximum edge count of an n-vertex edge-ordered graph containing no order-preserving copy of F.

**Source.** Gaurav Sunil Kucheriya. *Extremal Problems on Graphs with Additional Structure*. Charles University, 2026. Advisor(s): Mykhaylo Tyomkyn. [primary source](https://dspace.cuni.cz/handle/20.500.11956/213532) Location: §2 concluding remark C, p. 52; open-problems chapter p. 106.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2556, 2557, checked 7 October 2026: source retains the question; bounded searches found no matching resolution.

<a id="q2558"></a>

## Q2558. Polynomial chromatic bounds from induced matching treewidth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 16) · **Collection** 26

For every integer μ≥1, is there a polynomial p_μ such that every finite simple graph G with tree-μ(G)≤μ satisfies χ(G)≤p_μ(ω(G))?

**Context.** Origin for question 2558: Alon–Milanič–Rzążewski, November 2025 preprint. Setup for question 2558: For a tree decomposition T of a finite simple graph G, μ(T) is the largest size of an induced matching whose every edge has an endpoint in a common bag. The induced matching treewidth tree-μ(G) is min_T μ(T).

**Source.** Noga Alon; Martin Milanič; Paweł Rzążewski. *Induced Matching Treewidth and Tree-Independence Number, Revisited*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14869) Location: Question 16, p. 9.

## Q2559. Bounded geodetic counts on an infinite subsequence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Does there exist a constant c for which g(n)≤c for infinitely many positive integers n?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2559, 2560: Stober–Weiß (2023); final version September 2026. Setup for questions 2559, 2560: A finite connected simple graph is geodetic when every pair of vertices has a unique shortest path. Let g(n) count isomorphism classes of biconnected geodetic graphs on n vertices.

<a id="q2561"></a>

## Q2561. An isolated smallest edge gives bounded saturation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 59) · **Collection** 26

For every fixed finite edge-ordered graph G, is sat_m(n,e_0+G)=O(1) as n→∞?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2561: Bošković: Paris-Saclay PhD student; supervisors Sanjay Ramassamy and Cédric Boutillier; primary-paper attribution. Origin for question 2561: Bošković–Keszegh, August 2024 preprint. Setup for question 2561: Graphs are finite and simple. Copies preserve edge orders. sat_e(n,F) minimizes edges among F-free n-vertex edge-ordered graphs where adding any missing edge at any order position creates F. sat_m requires this only for a new smallest edge. ssat_e omits F-freeness but requires a new F-copy. e_0+G adds a disjoint edge smaller than every G-edge.

**Source.** Vladimir Bošković; Balázs Keszegh. *Saturation of Edge-Ordered Graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13742) Location: Conjecture 59, p. 31.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2561, checked 7 October 2026: source retains the question; bounded searches found no matching resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00457v2) · [2](https://www.combinatorics.org/ojs/index.php/eljc/article/download/v33i3p62/pdf/) · [3](https://vldboskovic.github.io/CV%20-%20VBoskovic%20nov25.pdf) · [4](https://www.normalesup.org/~ramassamy/)


<a id="q2562"></a>

## Q2562. Every tredoku pattern admits a digit filling

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Can the sub-lozenges of every tredoku pattern be labeled 1,…,9 so that each original tile and each nine-cell strand following a length-three run contains every label exactly once?

**Context.** Origin for question 2562: Blackburn, open problem 2; pattern definition due to Preece. Setup for question 2562: A tredoku pattern is ≥3 nonoverlapping lozenges on the triangular lattice, edge-connected and simply connected, remaining path-connected after any tile deletion. Every maximal run of edge-adjacent tiles across parallel shared edges has length 1 or 3. Subdivide each lozenge into a 3×3 grid.

**Source.** Simon R. Blackburn. *Tredoku Patterns*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13659) Location: Concluding remarks, item 2, p. 26; Definitions 1–3, pp. 2–3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2562, checked 7 October 2026: source retains the question; bounded searches found no matching resolution.

<a id="q2563"></a>

## Q2563. The sharp binomial treedepth bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 22) · **Collection** 26

For all k,t≥2, is max{td(G):td_2(G)≤k and G is P_t-free}=binom(⌊(t−1)/2⌋+k−1,⌊(t−1)/2⌋)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2563, 2564: Frederick Charles Illingworth. Illingworth: Cambridge PhD, The Chromatic Structure of Dense Graphs (2021); supervisor Andrew Thomason. Later primary paper. Origin for questions 2563, 2564: Hodor–Illingworth–Mazur, 2025 preprint. Setup for questions 2563, 2564: Graphs are finite and simple. td is treedepth. Set td_2(∅)=0; on a nonempty block, td_2(G)=1+min_v td_2(G−v); otherwise take the maximum over blocks, including bridge edges and isolated vertices. P_t denotes the t-vertex path; P_t-free means no induced P_t.

**Source.** Jędrzej Hodor; Freddie Illingworth; Tomasz Mazur. *Treedepth and 2-Treedepth in Graphs with No Long Induced Paths*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14727) Location: Conjecture 22, p. 19.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2563, 2564, checked 7 October 2026: source retains the question; bounded searches found no matching resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.combinatorics.org/ojs/index.php/eljc/article/download/v33i3p55/pdf/) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04445) · [3](https://sites.google.com/view/freddieillingworth) · [4](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/7f205e4f-b3f6-4159-9a08-30b13e8a09f2/content)

## Q2565. Recognizing tournaments of directed clique number two

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.1) · **Collection** 26

What is the computational complexity of deciding, for an input tournament T, whether ω⃗(T)≤2?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2565, 2566: Coulomb: Paris Cité PhD student, Still Coloring Digraphs (in progress); supervisors Pierre Charbit and Pierre Aboulker. Origin for questions 2565, 2566: Aubian–Coulomb; earlier Aboulker–Aubian–Charbit–Lopes questions. Setup for questions 2565, 2566: Digraphs are finite and loopless, without parallel arcs; opposite arcs are allowed. Tournaments have exactly one arc per vertex pair. D^≺ is the undirected graph of arcs pointing backward in vertex order ≺. Set ω⃗(D)=min_≺ω(D^≺); χ⃗(D) is the minimum number of acyclic parts partitioning V(D). Substitution replaces a vertex by a digraph with the same external adjacencies.

**Source.** Guillaume Aubian; Samuel Coulomb. *Clique Number of Tournaments II*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07481) Location: Problem 7.1, p. 18.

## Q2648. Nonreal RSK eigenvalues

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.59) · **Collection** 27

Whenever ℓ(σ),ℓ(π)≥3, must T_{σ,π} have a nonreal eigenvalue?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2648, 2649, 2650: Illinois Urbana-Champaign PhD; director Alexander Yong. Origin for questions 2648, 2649, 2650: Joint Stelzer–Yong (2024); thesis Chapter 6. Setup for questions 2648, 2649, 2650: For positive compositions σ,π of d, T_{σ,π} acts on ℂ-monomials z^A with row/column sums σ,π. For row-insertion matrix RSK(A)=(P,Q), with contents σ,π respectively, T(z^A)=∏\_c det(z_{ij})\_{i∈P_c,j∈Q_c}, using corresponding tableau columns in increasing order. Reduced means max(σ)+max(π)≤d; triangular means min(ℓ(σ),ℓ(π))=2 and σ₁=π₁.

**Source.** Ada Mead Stelzer. *Gröbner Crystal Structures: A Combinatorial Approach to Equivariant Homological Invariants*. University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign, 2026. Advisor(s): Alexander Yong. [primary source](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1qOUxqTuWbgRc9CzaTZZLFj1iXOyV7Rz2/view) Location: Conjecture 6.59, p. 152; journal 5.5.

<a id="q2653"></a>

## Q2653. Recognizing arc-weighted degeneracy

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 38) · **Collection** 27

Determine the computational complexity of deciding whether an input graph G is arc-weighted f-degenerate, given its vertexwise nonnegative integer bounds f in binary.

**Context.** Origin for question 2653: Authors’ stated problem. Setup for question 2653: Graphs are finite, simple and undirected. An orientation D with positive integer arc weights w is arc-weighted acyclic if every subdigraph D′ containing an arc has an arc u→v with w(u→v)>Σ\_{v→x∈E(D′)}w(v→x). G is arc-weighted f-degenerate if such an orientation has Σ\_{v→x∈E(D)}w(v→x)≤f(v) for all v.

**Source.** Huan Zhou; Jialu Zhu; Xuding Zhu. *Arc-Weighted Acyclic Orientation of Graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14448) Location: Question 38, p. 25; Definitions 3–5, pp. 2–3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2653, checked 7 October 2026: September journal Question 38 remains; no later classification found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.15853)

## Q2658. Polynomial prefactor for tile counts

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6) · **Collection** 27

For each d≥1, is t_{n,d}=O_d(n^{C(d)}3^{n^d/3}) for some finite C(d), and can C(d)=d−1 be used?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2657, 2658: Authors’ stated problem. Setup for questions 2657, 2658: A nonempty S⊆ℤ^d is a tile if disjoint translates of S partition ℤ^d. Let t_{n,d} count tiles contained in [n]^d, without quotienting translations.

**Source.** Itai Benjamini; Gady Kozma; Elad Tzalik. *The Number of Tiles of ℤ^d*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14513) Location: Conjecture 6, p. 6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2657, 2658, checked 7 October 2026: August journal retains these questions; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.07956)


<a id="q2659"></a>

## Q2659. Complete graphs maximize modularity edit distance

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 27

Does every n-vertex graph G, n≥4, satisfy δ(G)≤⌊n/2⌋+1?

**Context.** Origin for question 2659: Authors’ stated problem. Setup for question 2659: For a finite simple graph G with m>0 edges, q\*(G)=max_𝒫 Σ\_{A∈𝒫}(e_G(A)/m−(Σ\_{v∈A}deg_G(v))²/(4m²)), maximizing over vertex partitions. Set q\*(G)=0 when m=0. Let δ(G) be the fewest edge additions/deletions on its fixed vertex set needed to obtain positive modularity.

**Source.** Colin McDiarmid; Fiona Skerman. *On Graphs with Modularity Zero or Near-Zero*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/12644) Location: Question 4 and Theorem 1, pp. 2–3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2659, checked 7 October 2026: August journal and July v3 retain Question 4; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.06875)


<a id="q2660"></a>

## Q2660. Pairwise maximum connectivity for star puzzles

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 43) · **Collection** 27

If FS(X,Star_n) is connected, must every pair of distinct vertices σ,ρ admit min{deg(σ),deg(ρ)} internally vertex-disjoint σ–ρ paths?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2660, 2661: Local connectivity: Krishnan–Li. Component classification: Colin Defant. Setup for questions 2660, 2661: For finite simple n-vertex graphs X,Y, FS(X,Y) has bijections σ:V(X)→V(Y) as vertices. Adjacent bijections differ by swapping a,b∈V(X) with ab∈E(X) and σ(a)σ(b)∈E(Y). Star_n=K_{1,n−1}; P_n,C_n are the n-vertex path and cycle. Connectivity means vertex connectivity, with κ(K_m)=m−1.

**Source.** Neil Krishnan; Rupert Li. *Vertex Connectivity of Friends-and-Strangers Graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/15082) Location: Conjecture 43, p. 33.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2660, 2661, checked 7 October 2026: August journal retains both targets; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21334)

<a id="q2662"></a>

## Q2662. Smallest matching-scheme spectral gap

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 29) · **Collection** 27

For every n≥5 and μ⊢n with μ≠1^n, is λ₁(A_μ)−λ₂(A_μ)≥2n−1, the gap attained by μ=(2,1^{n−2})?

**Context.** Origin for question 2662: Authors’ stated problem. Setup for question 2662: For μ⊢n, A_μ indexes perfect matchings of K_{2n}; two are related when their multiset union has cycle lengths 2μ₁,2μ₂,…, counting shared edges as 2-cycles. Eigenvalues are ordered algebraically with multiplicity. The S_{2n}-action relabels vertices.

**Source.** Himanshu Gupta; Allen Herman; Alice Lacaze-Masmonteil; Roghayeh Maleki; Karen Meagher. *On the Second Largest Eigenvalue of Certain Graphs in the Perfect Matching Association Scheme*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/15092) Location: Conjecture 29, p. 37; gap formula p. 16.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2662, checked 7 October 2026: August journal retains these conjectures; no later resolution located.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Every nonidentity perfect-matching relation has adjacency gap at least 2n-1 for all n>=5. Scope: All n>=5 and all partitions mu!=1^n. Assumptions: Unnormalized adjacency eigenvalues ordered algebraically with multiplicity, exactly as in the handoff. Remaining: Nothing remains at the original minimum-gap scope; the exact second eigenvalue for every relation is not claimed. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.17135)


<a id="q2663"></a>

## Q2663. Three-quarter oriented-cycle threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1) · **Collection** 27

Is there an absolute C such that t(n)≥3n/4−C for all sufficiently large n?

**Context.** Origin for question 2663: Authors’ stated problem. Setup for question 2663: On K_n, OMaker moves first, orienting one unused edge per turn; OBreaker then orients between one and b unused edges. Play ends when all edges are oriented. OMaker wins exactly when the final tournament contains a directed cycle. Let t(n) be the largest integer b for which OMaker can force a win.

**Source.** Anita Liebenau; Abdallah Saffidine; Jeffrey Yang. *An Improved Upper Bound on the Threshold Bias of the Oriented-Cycle Game*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14172) Location: Conjecture 4.1, p. 23.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2663, checked 7 October 2026: September journal/v2 retain the conjecture; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16770)


<a id="q2664"></a>

## Q2664. Balanced whirling of repeated surjections

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.11) · **Collection** 27

Let n,k,m≥1 with n≥mk. On functions f:[n]→[k] with every fibre of size ≥m, w_i repeatedly increments f(i) cyclically modulo k until admissible again. Does every orbit of w_n∘⋯∘w₁ average |f⁻¹(j)| to n/k for each j∈[k]?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2664, 2665: Authors’ stated problem.

**Source.** Michael Joseph; James Propp; Tom Roby. *Whirling Injections, Surjections, and Other Functions between Finite Sets*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.46298/dmtcs.14126) Location: Conjecture 2.11, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2664, 2665, checked 7 October 2026: December v6 retains both conjectures; no later resolution located.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Every repeated-surjection whirling orbit has each fibre average n/k, proved by an explicit coboundary. Scope: All n,k,m>=1 with n>=mk, every orbit and every fibre. Assumptions: None changed; any coordinate update order is also allowed. Remaining: Nothing remains at the original orbit-average scope. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.02411v6)


<a id="q2665"></a>

## Q2665. Asymptotic homomesy on a slab

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.28) · **Collection** 27

For each integer n≥1 and real r with 0≤r≤n/2, put P={x∈[0,1]^n:r≤Σx_i≤n−r}. The toggle t_i replaces x_i by max(0,r−s)+min(1,n−r−s)−x_i, where s=Σ\_{j≠i}x_j. For every permutation π and x∈P, does N⁻¹Σ\_{j=0}^{N−1}Σ\_i(T_π^j x)\_i→n/2, where T_π=t_{π(n)}∘⋯∘t_{π(1)}?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2664, 2665: Authors’ stated problem.

**Source.** Michael Joseph; James Propp; Tom Roby. *Whirling Injections, Surjections, and Other Functions between Finite Sets*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.46298/dmtcs.14126) Location: Conjecture 2.28, p. 12.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2664, 2665, checked 7 October 2026: December v6 retains both conjectures; no later resolution located.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Every real slab orbit has average n/2, with uniform error <=(n-r)/(2N). Scope: Every integer n>=1, real 0<=r<=n/2, permutation pi, point x in the slab, and integer N>=1. Assumptions: None; no rationality, periodicity, or almost-everywhere qualification. Remaining: Nothing remains at the original real-slab time-average scope. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.02411v6)

## Q2751. Unimodality of q-Fibonomials

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.6) · **Collection** 28

For all positive integers m,n, is the coefficient sequence of Bₘ,ₙ(q) unimodal?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2751, 2752: Vienna doctorate; supervisor Michael Schlosser. Origin for questions 2751, 2752: Joint Bergeron–Ceballos–Küstner questions (2020), repeated in the thesis. Setup for questions 2751, 2752: Let F₀=0,F₁=1,Fᵣ₊₂=Fᵣ₊₁+Fᵣ, [a]q=1+q+⋯+q^(a−1), and Bₘ,ₙ(q)=∏ᵢ₌₁^(m+n)[Fᵢ]q/(∏ᵢ₌₁^m[Fᵢ]q∏ᵢ₌₁^n[Fᵢ]q).

**Source.** Josef Küstner. *Elliptic combinatorics of lattice paths, domino tilings and rook placements*. University of Vienna, 2022. Advisor(s): Michael Schlosser. [primary source](https://utheses-gateway.univie.ac.at/apiFulltext/document/get/file/64115) Location: Conjecture 4.6, p. 66.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2751, 2752, checked 7 October 2026: May 2026 paper reaffirms both; unimodality is proved only for min(m,n)≤3.

**Further links.** [1](https://utheses.univie.ac.at/detail/64115) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.12785) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12822)

## Q2753. Bipartite K₂-hypohamiltonian graphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Does a bipartite K₂-hypohamiltonian graph exist?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2753, 2754, 2755: KU Leuven PhD; supervisors Jan Goedgebeur and Carol T. Zamfirescu. Origin for questions 2753, 2754, 2755: K₂ questions: thesis synthesis of joint work; snark questions predate the thesis. Setup for questions 2753, 2754, 2755: Graphs are finite, simple and connected. K₂-hypohamiltonian means 3-connected, non-Hamiltonian, with every adjacent-pair deletion Hamiltonian. A permutation snark is cubic, not 3-edge-colourable, and has a spanning 2-factor consisting of two induced cycles.

**Source.** Jarne Renders. *Studying Cycles with Computational Graph Theory*. KU Leuven, 2025. Advisor(s): Jan Goedgebeur; Carol T. Zamfirescu. [primary source](https://www.st.fmph.uniba.sk/~lukac133/thesis_Jarne_Renders.pdf) Location: §8.1, p. 175; §3.1, p. 53.

## Q2756. Homogeneous vertex-coloured digraphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Classify all finite vertex-coloured ultrahomogeneous digraphs.

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2756, 2757: Darmstadt doctorate; supervisor Pascal Schweitzer. Origin for questions 2756, 2757: Unnumbered future-work questions after the thesis classifications. Setup for questions 2756, 2757: Ultrahomogeneous means every colour-preserving isomorphism between finite induced substructures extends to a colour-preserving automorphism. Digraphs are loopless, with at most one arc per ordered vertex pair; opposite arcs are permitted. A complete coloured digraph assigns a colour to every ordered pair of distinct vertices.

**Source.** Eda Kaja. *Classifications at the intersection of group and graph theory*. TU Darmstadt, 2025. Advisor(s): Pascal Schweitzer. [primary source](https://tuprints.ulb.tu-darmstadt.de/server/api/core/bitstreams/ac0ab29e-ac55-4a07-990f-ad4f1ffc6609/content) Location: §3.5, p. 79, first paragraph.

## Q2758. Three-ultrahomogeneous coloured graphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Classify all finite vertex-coloured 3-ultrahomogeneous graphs.

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2758, 2759: Darmstadt doctorate; supervisor Pascal Schweitzer. Origin for questions 2758, 2759: Thesis future-work question; classical 4-tuple-regular gap explicitly reaffirmed there. Setup for questions 2758, 2759: Graphs are finite, simple and undirected. A vertex-coloured graph is 3-ultrahomogeneous if every colour-preserving isomorphism between induced subgraphs on at most three vertices extends to an automorphism. Ultrahomogeneous removes the three-vertex bound. An uncoloured graph is 4-tuple regular if |∩\_{v∈S}N(v)| depends only on the isomorphism type of G[S], for every S⊆V with |S|≤4.

**Source.** Thomas Heinrich Schneider. *Structure theory and algorithms for highly regular colored graphs*. TU Darmstadt, 2025. Advisor(s): Pascal Schweitzer. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1360543406/34) Location: Chapter 7, p. 100.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2758, 2759, checked 7 October 2026: thesis and 2025 joint SIAM paper retain these gaps; no later solution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.mathematik.tu-darmstadt.de/didaktik/did/did_informationen/did_aktuelles/news_didaktik_detailseite_15616.de.jsp) · [2](https://tuprints.ulb.tu-darmstadt.de/29557/) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1137/23M1565309) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.01058)

## Q2762. Treewidth from frustration dimension

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Does a function f:ℕ₀→ℕ₀ satisfy tw(G)≤f(d(G)) for every finite simple graph G?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2762, 2763: Author’s concluding questions. Setup for questions 2762, 2763: For connected finite simple G with a cycle, let d(G) maximize, over nonnegative vertex weights s, edge lengths l and signs σ:E→{±1} with a negative cycle, the minimum dim span{y_v} among optimizers of max Σ\_v s_v‖y_v‖² subject to ‖y_u−σ(uv)y_v‖≤l_uv, with vectors in any finite-dimensional real Euclidean space. Set d(∅)=d(K₁)=0, d(T)=1 for nontrivial trees, and take maxima over nonempty component sets.

**Source.** Uwe Schwerdtfeger. *The Algebraic Frustration Dimension of a Graph*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/12461) Location: §8, p. 23; Definition 3, p. 5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2762, 2763, checked 7 October 2026: September journal leaves both open; no subsequent resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.combinatorics.org/ojs/index.php/eljc/article/download/v33i3p57/pdf/)

<a id="q2764"></a>

## Q2764. Unicyclic invariants determined by the CSF

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 63) · **Collection** 28

If connected unicyclic graphs G,H each have either a 3-cycle and odd order, or a cycle of length ≥4, does X_G=X_H force equal degree sequences, equal numbers of nontrivial attached rooted trees, and equal numbers of internal edges?

**Context.** Origin for question 2764: Authors’ Conjecture 63. Setup for question 2764: For finite simple G, X_G=Σ\_κ∏\_{v∈V(G)}x_{κ(v)}, summed over proper colourings κ:V(G)→ℕ. A connected unicyclic graph has one cycle. Its attached rooted trees are components after deleting cycle edges; a tree is nontrivial if it has >1 vertex. An internal edge has both endpoints of degree ≥2.

**Source.** Aram Bingham; Lisa Johnston; Colin Lawson; Rosa Orellana; Jianping Pan; Chelsea Sato. *The Chromatic Symmetric Function for Unicyclic Graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/15119) Location: Conjecture 63, p. 41.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2764, checked 7 October 2026: July journal retains it; later arbitrary-girth equal-CSF examples do not resolve these invariants.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06486) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09563) · [3](https://automathon.org/index.php/research/)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5) · **Collection** 28

## Q2767. Exact large-order XOR-square clique number

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 28

For each integer k≥1, is f₂(n,k)=⌊n/k⌋+(k/2) binom(2k,k)−k for all sufficiently large n?

**Context.** Origin for question 2767: Authors’ conjectures. Setup for question 2767: For positive integers ℓ,n,k with n≥k, f_ℓ(n,k) is the largest size of a family of ℓ-tuples of k-subsets of [n] such that any two distinct tuples are disjoint in an odd number of coordinates. Equivalently it is the clique number of the ℓ-fold XOR power of KG(n,k).

**Source.** Zoltán Füredi; András Imolay; Ádám Schweitzer. *Clique Number of XOR-Powers of Kneser Graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14969) Location: Conjecture 2, p. 3.

<a id="q2821"></a>

## Q2821. Monotileability of arbitrary countable groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question I.3.1) · **Collection** 29

For every countable group G and finite F⊂G, is F contained in a finite tile?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2821: Joseph Paul MacManus. Splittings, tiles, and planarity: a trio of problems in geometric group theory. PhD, University of Oxford, 2025. Advisors: Panagiotis Papazoglou. Origin for question 2821: Chou–Weiss monotileability question. Setup for question 2821: A finite T⊂G is a tile if G=⊔\_{d∈D}dT for some D⊂G. A CCC sequence consists of such tilings (D_n,T_n) with 1∈D_n∩T_n, T_n⊆T_{n+1}, ⋃T_n=G, and each partition {dT_n:d∈D_n} refining the next.

**Source.** Joseph MacManus, Lawk Mineh. *Tiling in some nonpositively curved groups, May 2026 version*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.09545v2) Location: Introduction, p. 1; thesis Question I.3.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2821, checked 7 October 2026: The May 2026 version retains this target; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid%3A11d13945-2ddb-4159-8d79-bb29f9f36569) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09545) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1104.1231)


<a id="q2855"></a>

## Q2855. When simple designs span all real designs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem B) · **Collection** 29

Determine precisely which parameter tuples (q,n,k,t) satisfy span_R(D)=U.

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2855: VUB PhD; advisor Jan De Beule. Origin for question 2855: Thesis question; underlying richness condition appears in joint Kiermaier–Mannaert–Wassermann work. Setup for question 2855: Let q=1 or a prime power, and 0≤t≤k≤n. For q>1 let V=F_q^n and Ω be its k-subspaces. Let U be the real functions δ on Ω for which ∑\_{K⊇T}δ(K) is independent of the t-subspace T. Let D=U∩{0,1}^Ω, the simple t-designs with unrestricted replication. For q=1, use subsets of [n] instead.

**Source.** Jonathan Mannaert. *Boolean functions with a geometric domain: a place where algebra and combinatorics meet*. Vrije Universiteit Brussel, 2025. Advisor(s): Jan De Beule. [primary source](https://cris.vub.be/ws/portalfiles/portal/133307973/Jonathan_Mannaert_PhD_thesis.pdf) Location: Problem B.2.1, p. 142.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2855, checked 7 October 2026: The 2025 thesis and revised joint paper retain this classification problem; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://researchportal.vub.be/en/publications/boolean-functions-with-a-geometric-domain-a-place-where-algebra-a/) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07572) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jcta.2024.105979) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23465)

## Q2859. A non-extremal triple-array family

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 55) · **Collection** 29

For every prime power q, does an (r×c,v)-triple array exist with r=(q³−1)/(q−1), c=(q⁴−1)/(q−1), and v=(q⁴−1)(q³−1)/((q²−1)(q−1))?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2859, 2860: Authors’ non-extremal conjecture and strengthening of Agrawal’s construction. Setup for questions 2859, 2860: An (r×c,v)-triple array uses v symbols equally often, without repeats within a row or column, with constant row–row, column–column and row–column support intersections. Its unordered version specifies r row supports of size c and c column supports of size r, with the same intersection conditions and each symbol occurring in e supports of each kind.

**Source.** Alexey Gordeev; Lars-Daniel Öhman. *Resolvable Triple Arrays*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14977) Location: Conjecture 55, p. 32.

## Q2862. Separating generalized Ramsey and list-Ramsey growth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 15) · **Collection** 29

Are there fixed integers p≥3 and 2≤q≤binom(p,2) for which f_list(n,p,q) and f(n,p,q) have different orders of magnitude as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for question 2862: Authors’ generalized list-Ramsey question. Setup for question 2862: Let f(n,p,q) be the minimum number of edge colours on K_n so each K_p uses at least q colours. Let f_list(n,p,q) be the least s such that every assignment of s allowed colours to each edge admits such a colouring from its lists.

**Source.** Patrick Bennett; Michelle Delcourt; Lina Li; Luke Postle. *On Generalized Ramsey Numbers in the Non-Integral Regime*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14326) Location: Question 15, p. 12.

## Q2863. Ternary hole-growth bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 30) · **Collection** 29

Is HB_3(n+1)≤HB_3(n)+1 for every integer n≥1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2863, 2864: Authors’ ternary conjecture; Blanchet-Sadri et al.’s four-letter conjecture, reaffirmed here. Setup for questions 2863, 2864: A partial word uses letters and holes. Equal-length words are compatible if all positions where both have letters agree. Unbordered means no nonempty proper prefix is compatible with the equal-length suffix. Let HB_k(n) be the maximum holes in an unbordered length-n word over k letters; HB_∞(n)=HB_n(n).

**Source.** Aleksi Saarela; Aleksi Vanhatalo. *A Connection Between Unbordered Partial Words and Sparse Rulers*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13806) Location: Conjecture 30, p. 16.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2863, 2864, checked 7 October 2026: Both remain conjectures in February 2026; the four-letter growth counterexample does not settle either.

## Q2932. Coherent tilings for hyperbolic actions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.4) · **Collection** 30

Does every countable acylindrically hyperbolic group admit a CCC sequence of monotilings?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2932: Joseph Paul MacManus. Splittings, tiles, and planarity: a trio of problems in geometric group theory. PhD, University of Oxford, 2025. Advisors: Panagiotis Papazoglou. Origin for question 2932: MacManus–Mineh’s CCC question, following work of Gao–Jackson–Seward. Setup for question 2932: A finite T⊂G is a tile if G=⊔\_{d∈D}dT for some D⊂G. A CCC sequence consists of such tilings (D_n,T_n) with 1∈D_n∩T_n, T_n⊆T_{n+1}, ⋃T_n=G, and each partition {dT_n:d∈D_n} refining the next.

**Source.** Joseph MacManus, Lawk Mineh. *Tiling in some nonpositively curved groups, May 2026 version*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.09545v2) Location: Question 6.4, p. 16; CCC definition in Seward §1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2932, checked 7 October 2026: May 2026 Question 6.4 remains open; ordinary monotileability does not supply coherent tilings.

**Further links.** [1](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid%3A11d13945-2ddb-4159-8d79-bb29f9f36569) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09545) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1104.1231)

## Q2955. Intersecting linear-forest-free graphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 8.7) · **Collection** 30

For every linear forest H and k≥1, does a function f_(H,k) exist such that χ(G)≤f_(H,k)(ω(G)) whenever G is the edge-set intersection of k H-free graphs on the same vertex set?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2955, 2956: Waterloo PhD; advisors Sophie Spirkl and Rowan Yeats. Origin for questions 2955, 2956: Thesis conjectures: intersection work with Koerts–Spirkl; broadleaved work with LaGrange–Rundström–Spirkl. Setup for questions 2955, 2956: Graphs are finite and simple; H-free means induced-H-free. Write ω for clique number and χ\_r for the fewest vertex parts each inducing a K_r-free graph. An s-broadleaved forest/tree replaces each leaf of a forest/tree by a nonempty clique of size≤s−1, inheriting its neighbours.

**Source.** Aristotelis Chaniotis. *Preserving and Generalizing χ-boundedness*. 2025. [primary source](https://www.math.uwaterloo.ca/~kayeats/students/Chaniotis_Aristotelis.pdf) Location: Conjecture 8.7, p. 80.

## Q2957. Removing the complete-partition logarithm

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 30

**Context.** Origin for questions 2957, 2958: First: Axenovich–Martin; second: authors’ proposed asymptotic. Setup for questions 2957, 2958: Graphs are finite and simple. <a id="q2963"></a>

## Q2963. Exact cop bound for string graphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

What is the maximum cop number among intersection graphs of finite families of simple plane curves?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2963, 2964: First: intersection-graph question; second: Gromovikov–Kinnersley–Seamone. Setup for questions 2963, 2964: Graphs are finite, connected, simple and undirected. Cops choose starting vertices, then the visible robber chooses one; cops move first, with alternating turns. Each piece traverses an edge or stays. Sharing a vertex captures the robber. The cop number is the least number ensuring capture. In the fully active version every piece must traverse an edge on its turn.

**Source.** Sandip Das; Harmender Gahlawat. *On the Cop Number of String Graphs*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13317) Location: §5, p. 22.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2963, 2964, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026; no later resolution located in targeted searches.

## Q2969. Sharp uniform list-majority degree

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 23) · **Collection** 30

For every k≥2, does δ(G)≥k² ensure a 1/k-majority edge colouring from arbitrary lists of size k+1?

**Context.** Origin for question 2969: Authors’ list-colouring conjectures. Setup for question 2969: Graphs are finite and simple. Edge lists L(e) are finite subsets of colours C; α:C→(0,1). An α-majority list colouring assigns c(e)∈L(e) with at most α(c)d(v) incident edges of colour c at every vertex v. Lists are ε-excessive when ∑\_(c∈L(e))α(c)≥1+ε for every edge.

**Source.** Paweł Pękała; Jakub Przybyło. *On list extensions of the majority edge colourings*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13882) Location: Conjecture 23, p. 22.

## Q2972. Almost-Dirac directed Hamilton threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 15) · **Collection** 30

Let 1/2≤η(n)=o(n) with d=n/2−η integer, and p\*=η/n². Does every sequence H_n with δ⁰(H_n)≥d satisfy P(H_n∪D(n,p) is Hamiltonian)→1 when p/p\*→∞, while some such sequence has this probability→0 whenever p/p\*→0?

**Context.** Origin for question 2972: Authors’ directed critical-window conjecture. Setup for question 2972: Digraphs have no loops or parallel arcs, but opposite arcs are allowed. D(n,p) includes each ordered arc independently with probability p. Write δ⁰=min(δ⁺,δ⁻); all unions use the same n labelled vertices.

**Source.** Alberto Espuny Díaz; Richarlotte Valérà Razafindravola. *How Many Random Edges Make an Almost-Dirac Graph Hamiltonian?*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13608) Location: Conjecture 15, p. 12; Definition 1, p. 2.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 11) · **Collection** 30

**Context.** Origin for question 2973: Authors’ post-counterexample problem. Setup for question 2973: A strong 1/k-majority edge colouring of a finite simple graph assigns colours so that, for each edge e and colour c, at most (d(u)+d(v)−2)/k edges adjacent to e=uv have colour c. ## Q3058. Total-coloring a precolored matching

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.6) · **Collection** 31

For every finite simple graph G of maximum degree Δ and matching subgraph H, must every total-(Δ+3)-coloring of H in G extend to G?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3058, 3059: Owen Henderschedt. PhD, Auburn University, 2026. Advisors: Jessica McDonald. Origin for questions 3058, 3059: Orientation: Akbari et al.; extensions: Henderschedt–McDonald. Setup for questions 3058, 3059: Matching subgraphs have no isolated vertices. A total-k-coloring colors vertices and edges with [k], assigning distinct colors to adjacent or incident elements. “In G” also forbids equal-colored endpoints of G-edges absent from the precolored subgraph.

**Source.** Owen Henderschedt. *Orientations with forbidden out-degree and total-coloring extensions*. Auburn University, 2026. Advisor(s): Jessica McDonald. [primary source](https://etd.auburn.edu/handle/10415/10482) Location: Conjecture 1.6, p. 17; paper Conjecture 1.2, p. 2.

<a id="q3060"></a>

## Q3060. King-graph representation number

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 31

Determine R(King_{m,n}) for all integers n≥m≥5.

**Context.** Origin for questions 3060, 3061, 3062, 3063: The authors’ classification questions. Setup for questions 3060, 3061, 3062, 3063: On vertices [m]×[n], distinct (a,b),(c,d) are adjacent for King if max(|a−c|,|b−d|)=1; Rook if a=c or b=d; Bishop if |a−c|=|b−d|; Knight if {|a−c|,|b−d|}={1,2}. R(G) is the least k≥1 admitting a word containing each vertex k times, whose two-letter subword alternates exactly for adjacent pairs.

**Source.** Sergey Kitaev; Artem Pyatkin. *On the representation number of chessboard graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00236-026-00547-w) Location: Final journal §3, first bullet, p. 13.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3060, 3061, 3062, 3063, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: September18 journal §3 retains these questions; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22311) · [2](https://spider-v.science.strath.ac.uk/sergey.kitaev/publications.html)

Setup for questions 3060, 3061, 3062, 3063: On vertices [m]×[n], distinct (a,b),(c,d) are adjacent for King if max(|a−c|,|b−d|)=1; Rook if a=c or b=d; Bishop if |a−c|=|b−d|; Knight if {|a−c|,|b−d|}={1,2}. R(G) is the least k≥1 admitting a word containing each vertex k times, whose two-letter subword alternates exactly for adjacent pairs.

**Source.** Sergey Kitaev; Artem Pyatkin. *On the representation number of chessboard graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00236-026-00547-w) Location: Final journal §3, fourth bullet, p. 13.

Setup for question 3065: For perfect matchings M,N on [n], N≼M means every nonincreasing positive integer sequence (d₁,…,dₙ) realized by a simple labelled graph containing M is also realized by one containing N.
