## Q3066. The equitable chromatic threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2) · **Collection** 31

Must every connected graph G, other than a complete graph, an odd cycle, or K_{2m+1,2m+1}, admit a proper equitable k-coloring for every integer k≥Δ(G)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3066, 3067: First: Chen–Lih–Wu (1994). Second: Zhang–Zhang (2021); repeated in this paper. Setup for questions 3066, 3067: Graphs are finite and simple. An equitable k-coloring partitions V(G) into k classes, possibly empty, whose sizes differ by at most one. Proper means each class is independent. A graph is d-degenerate if each nonempty subgraph has a vertex of degree at most d.

**Source.** Yuping Gao; Allan Lo; Songling Shan. *Equitable tree colouring of graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13606) Location: Conjecture 1.2, p. 2.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 13) · **Collection** 31

For every finite simple graph G on [ℓ], is there a single polynomial P_G(q,z) agreeing with χ(A_G^q,z) at every prime power q and satisfying lim_{q→1} P_G(q,q^t)/(q−1)^ℓ=χ(G,t)?

## Q3072. Bipartite deletion with sublinear independence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.8) · **Collection** 31

Determine d₂(K_q) for every integer q≥6.

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3072: Ce Chen. Extremal Problems in Graphs and Combinatorial Games. PhD, University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign, 2026. Advisors: József Balogh. Origin for question 3072: The authors’ new Ramsey–Turán deletion problem; Ce Chen’s doctoral context is separate. <a id="q3157"></a>

## Q3157. DP-painting from a Type-1 orientation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 37) · **Collection** 32

Must G be f-DP-paintable?

**Context.** Origin for question 3157: Authors’ Type-1 question; Type-2 recognition is delivered Q2653 and is excluded. Setup for question 3157: Let D orient a finite simple graph G, with positive integer arc weights w. It is Type-1 acyclic when no nonempty arc-subdigraph has equal weighted indegree and outdegree at every vertex. Set f(v)=1+Σ\_{v→u}w(vu). In the DP-painting game, each uncoloured v starts with f(v) tokens. Each round Lister offers disjoint colour lists L(v), costing |L(v)| available tokens, and a matching between lists on each edge. Painter permanently colours a subset, choosing one offered colour per vertex with no matched pair. Painter must colour all vertices before any uncoloured vertex exhausts its tokens.

**Source.** Huan Zhou; Jialu Zhu; Xuding Zhu. *Arc-weighted acyclic orientation of graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/14448) Location: Question 37, p. 25; DP-painting game in §2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3157, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: September25 final journal Question 37 retains the target; no later resolution located.

## Q3158. Blowing up linearly unavoidable families

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 7) · **Collection** 32

For each fixed k≥1, if F is linearly unavoidable and has uniformly bounded mad, must {D[k]:D∈F} be linearly unavoidable?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3158, 3159: Authors’ Problem 6 and Conjecture 7; other source targets already delivered as Q1657–1658 are excluded. Setup for questions 3158, 3159: For a finite acyclic digraph D, R(D) is the least N forcing D as a subdigraph in every N-vertex tournament. mad(D)=max_{∅≠H⊆D}2|A(H)|/|V(H)|. A family F is linearly unavoidable if R(D)≤C|V(D)| uniformly on F. D[k] replaces each vertex by k independent twins and each arc by all k² corresponding arcs.

**Source.** Pierre Aboulker; Frédéric Havet; William Lochet; Raul Lopes; Lucas Picasarri-Arrieta; Clément Rambaud. *Blow-ups and extensions of trees in tournaments*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13540) Location: Conjecture 7, p. 4.

## Q3161. Bounding the dichromatic number of poset tournaments

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 14) · **Collection** 32

Is there a function f such that χ⃗(T)≤f(ω⃗(T)) for every poset tournament T?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3161, 3162, 3163: Authors’ Conjecture 14 and Questions 23–24. Setup for questions 3161, 3162, 3163: For a tournament T, ω⃗(T)=min_≺ω(T^≺), where T^≺ is its undirected backward-arc graph; χ⃗(T) is the least number of acyclic vertex parts. Let pod(T) be the least number of transitive acyclic spanning subdigraphs of T whose arc sets cover A(T). A poset tournament admits an order ≺ such that x≺y≺z and arcs x→y,y→z imply x→z. H-free means no subtournament isomorphic to H.

**Source.** Pierre Aboulker; Logan Crew; Julien Duron; Xinyue Fan; Hugo Jacob; Rémy Kimbrough; Hidde Koerts; Benjamin Moore; Sophie Spirkl; Stéphan Thomassé. *Decomposing tournaments into comparability graphs*. 2026. Let pod(T) be the least number of transitive acyclic spanning subdigraphs of T whose arc sets cover A(T). A poset tournament admits an order ≺ such that x≺y≺z and arcs x→y,y→z imply x→z. H-free means no subtournament isomorphic to H.

**Source.** Pierre Aboulker; Logan Crew; Julien Duron; Xinyue Fan; Hugo Jacob; Rémy Kimbrough; Hidde Koerts; Benjamin Moore; Sophie Spirkl; Stéphan Thomassé. *Decomposing tournaments into comparability graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07748v1) Location: Question 23, p. 9.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3161, 3162, 3163, checked 8 October 2026: The current June 2026 preprint retains these questions.


<a id="q3163"></a>

## Q3163. Computing partial-order decomposition number

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 24) · **Collection** 32

Determine the computational complexity of computing pod(T) from a finite tournament T.

**Context.** Origin for questions 3161, 3162, 3163: Authors’ Conjecture 14 and Questions 23–24. Setup for questions 3161, 3162, 3163: For a tournament T, ω⃗(T)=min_≺ω(T^≺), where T^≺ is its undirected backward-arc graph; χ⃗(T) is the least number of acyclic vertex parts. Let pod(T) be the least number of transitive acyclic spanning subdigraphs of T whose arc sets cover A(T). A poset tournament admits an order ≺ such that x≺y≺z and arcs x→y,y→z imply x→z. H-free means no subtournament isomorphic to H.

**Source.** Pierre Aboulker; Logan Crew; Julien Duron; Xinyue Fan; Hugo Jacob; Rémy Kimbrough; Hidde Koerts; Benjamin Moore; Sophie Spirkl; Stéphan Thomassé. *Decomposing tournaments into comparability graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07748v1) Location: Question 24, p. 9.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3161, 3162, 3163, checked 8 October 2026: The current June 2026 preprint retains these questions.


<a id="q3164"></a>

## Q3164. Polynomial dichromatic bounds under substitution

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 12) · **Collection** 32

Does substitution closure preserve polynomial χ⃗-boundedness of hereditary tournament classes?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3164, 3165, 3166: Authors’ Question 12 and Conjectures 19,53; later papers settle different twin-width and tournament-substitution targets. Setup for questions 3164, 3165, 3166: All digraphs are finite, loopless, without parallel or opposite arcs. Let ω⃗(D)=min_≺ω(D^≺), with D^≺ the backward-arc graph; χ⃗(D) is the least number of acyclic vertex parts. Classes are hereditary. χ⃗-bounded means χ⃗(D)≤f(ω⃗(D)) uniformly; polynomially means polynomial f. Substitution replaces a vertex by a digraph, preserving all external in-/out-adjacencies and nonadjacencies. A BST-order is an inorder traversal of a rooted binary tree on V(T), each vertex having only inneighbours in its left subtree and outneighbours in its right subtree.

**Source.** Pierre Aboulker; Guillaume Aubian; Pierre Charbit; Raul Lopes. *Clique number of tournaments*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/12557) Location: Question 12, p. 11.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3164, 3165, 3166, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: these targets remain open; the established tournament substitution theorem gives no polynomial bound.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07481v1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07748v1)

## Q3168. When sparse classes tie domination to two-independence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 32

Characterize the sparse monotone classes C admitting h:ℕ₀→ℕ₀ such that γ(G)≤h(α₂(G)) for every G∈C.

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3168: Lukas Michel. Putting the pieces together: from graph decompositions to Ramsey theory. PhD, 2026, University of Oxford. Advisors: Alex Scott. Origin for question 3168: Authors’ Problem 2, explicitly retained for sparse monotone classes; found through Michel’s doctoral genealogy. Setup for question 3168: Graphs are finite and simple. γ(G) is the minimum dominating-set size; α₂(G) is the largest vertex-set size with pairwise distances greater than two. A sparse monotone class is closed under subgraphs and has uniformly bounded average degree.

**Source.** Marthe Bonamy; Zdeněk Dvořák; Lukas Michel; David Mikšaník. *Characterization of sparse monotone graph classes with bounded domination-to-2-independence ratio*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15082v1) Location: Problem 2, p. 2; explicitly open sparse-monotone specialization, p. 6.

<a id="q3188"></a>

## Q3188. Two-action antagonistic and zero-sum agreement

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 15) · **Collection** 32

If |S|=2, is z(h)=O_AA(h)₁−O_AA(h)₂ for every h≥0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3188, 3189: Conjecture 15 and §6 Problem 2, p. 12, in the authors’ primary paper. Setup for questions 3188, 3189: For finite nonempty S⊂N_{>0}, players alternate removing a legal s∈S from a heap h and adding s to their own score; play stops when h&lt;min S. Backward induction maximizes each mover’s final score. Ties maximize (F) or minimize (A) the opponent’s score. Let O_X(h) be the ordered score pair for profile X∈{FF,FA,AF,AA}, with the first letter describing the starting player. Define z(h)=max_{s∈S,s≤h}(s−z(h−s)), with z(h)=0 for h&lt;min S.

**Source.** Anjali Bhagat, Tanmay Kulkarni, Urban Larsson and Divya Murali. *Tie-breaking in self interest cumulative subtraction games*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.24280v2) Location: Conjecture 15, p. 12; Definition 2, pp. 4–5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3188, 3189, checked 8 October 2026: Current v2 checked; no later resolution located by 8 October 2026. The separate Conjecture 4 is excluded following a computed counterexample.

**Results.**

- **Proved** (B9: proved): Proved for every two-move set: antagonistic play coincides with zero-sum play. [Report](../../solutions/b9-2026-10-08/PROOF-Q3188.md).

**Source.** Anjali Bhagat, Tanmay Kulkarni, Urban Larsson and Divya Murali. *Tie-breaking in self interest cumulative subtraction games*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.24280v2) Location: §6, Problem 2, p. 12, read with Conjecture 17.

## Q3251. When a larger coloring bias hurts

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.22) · **Collection** 33

For positive integers m₁>m₂ with m₁+1 not divisible by m₂+1, must some finite simple G satisfy χ′\_g(G;m₁,1)>χ′\_g(G;m₂,1)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3251: Runze Wang. PhD, 2026, University of Memphis. Advisors: David Grynkiewicz. Origin for question 3251: Wang’s conjecture, also in his earlier paper. Setup for question 3251: On a finite simple graph, Maker moves first, properly coloring exactly m uncolored edges per turn; Breaker colors one. Maker wins when all edges are colored, even before completing a turn; otherwise inability to move loses for Maker. Let χ′\_g(G;m,1) be the least palette size guaranteeing Maker a win.

**Source.** Runze Wang. *Graph Labelings and Structures, Sumsets, and the Connections Between Them*. University of Memphis, 2026. Advisor(s): David Grynkiewicz. [primary source](https://digitalcommons.memphis.edu/etd/3943/) Location: Conjecture 2.22, p. 26.

## Q3254. Relative densities of ordered cycle-tails

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 13) · **Collection** 33

## Q3256. Strong-hub pebbling on cycles

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1) · **Collection** 33

For every n≥3, is h\*\_s(C_n)=2^k+2^{k−1}−3 when n=2k, and 2^{k+1}−3 when n=2k+1?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3256: see question 3251. Origin for question 3256: Wang’s conjecture. Setup for question 3256: In a finite simple connected graph, a nonempty U is a strong hub if every vertex pair has a path whose internal vertices lie in U. A pebbling move removes two pebbles at one vertex and adds one at a neighbor. h\*\_s(G) is the least t such that every t-pebble placement can reach one pebble on each vertex of some strong hub.

**Source.** Runze Wang. *Strong hub cover pebbling number*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05170v1) Location: Conjecture 4.1, p. 11.

## Q3258. Rotation wins the iid permutation game

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.38) · **Collection** 33

For k≥3 and iid continuous U_i, let H_σ=min{t≥k:st(U_{t−k+1},…,U_t)=σ}. Must Pr(H_σ&lt;H_{σ\_kσ₁…σ\_{k−1}})<1/2 for every σ∈S_k?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3258, 3259, 3260, 3261: Yixin Lin. PhD, 2024, Dartmouth College. Advisors: Sergi Elizalde. Origin for questions 3258, 3259, 3260, 3261: Chapter 2 thesis conjectures; iid target is joint Elizalde–Lin work. Setup for questions 3258, 3259, 3260, 3261: st replaces distinct entries by their relative ranks. For the Markov questions fix k≥2. On S_k, start X₀ uniformly and set P(σ,τ)=1/k if st(σ₂…σ\_k)=st(τ₁…τ\_{k−1}), zero otherwise; T_σ=min{t≥0:X_t=σ}.

**Source.** Yixin Lin. *Penney’s game for permutations*. Dartmouth College, 2024. Advisor(s): Sergi Elizalde. [primary source](https://digitalcommons.dartmouth.edu/dissertations/258/) Location: Conjecture 1.38, p. 45; final paper 6.1, p. 23.

## Q3262. Linear-in-s exponents modulo a prime square

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.4) · **Collection** 33

Does an absolute c>0 exist such that |F|=O_{p,L}(n^{cs}) for every such family as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for question 3262: Babai–Frankl–Kutin–Štefankovič question, retained by Xu–Yip. Setup for question 3262: Fix a prime p and nonempty L⊆{0,…,p²−1}, |L|=s. A family F⊆2^[n] is L-avoiding and L-intersecting modulo p² if |A| mod p²∉L for A∈F and |A∩B| mod p²∈L for distinct A,B∈F.

**Source.** Zixiang Xu; Chi Hoi Yip. *Sperner systems with restricted differences*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.02409v4) Location: Question 6.4, v4 p. 19; final p. 22.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3262, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: July’s corrected v4 and final Question 6.4 agree; interval L is proved.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.5070/C66165690) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/kyle-chi-hoi-yip/research)

## Q3263. A linear lower bound for slicing cube edges

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 33

Does an absolute c>0 exist such that every collection of affine hyperplanes slicing all edges of {−1,1}^n has at least cn members for every integer n≥1?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3263: Zixuan Xu. Extremal Hyperplane Problems on the Hypercube. PhD, 2026, Massachusetts Institute of Technology. Advisors: Lisa Sauermann. Origin for question 3263: Classical slicing conjecture, also called Paterson’s conjecture; found through Xu’s MIT thesis. Setup for question 3263: An affine hyperplane slices a cube edge if it meets the relative interior in exactly one point; containing the whole edge does not count.

**Source.** Lisa Sauermann; Zixuan Xu. *Improved lower bound for hypercube edge slicing*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16592v1) Location: Introduction, p. 1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3263, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: v1 remains current; February’s improved upper bound does not resolve the linear lower bound.

## Q3266. Exact threshold numbers of spiders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.3) · **Collection** 33

Determine Θ(T) for every tree T having exactly one vertex of degree at least three.

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3266: see question 3251. Origin for question 3266: Wang’s problem; found through his dissertation-linked research list. Setup for question 3266: For a finite simple graph G, Θ(G) is the least positive integer k permitting real vertex ranks r(v) and thresholds θ₁<⋯<θ\_k such that, for distinct u,v, uv is an edge exactly when |{i:θ\_i≤r(u)+r(v)}| is odd.

**Source.** Runze Wang. *Threshold numbers of some graphs*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12063v3) Location: Problem 4.3, p. 15.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3266, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: v3 remains current; no general spider formula located.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.2) · **Collection** 33

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1) · **Collection** 34

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3320: see question 3318. Origin for question 3320: Joint-paper questions, also in thesis §3.5. ## Q3330. Maximal hypertriangulations have maximum size

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture C) · **Collection** 34

Must all maximal level-k hypertriangulations of A have the same number of triangles?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3330: Alexey Igorevich Garber. Графы линейных операторов и билипишицевы классы множеств Делоне [Graphs of linear operators and bi-Lipschitz classes of Delone sets]. Candidate of physical and mathematical sciences (author CV calls this PhD), Steklov Mathematical Institute, Russian Academy of Sciences. Advisors: Nikolay Petrovich Dolbilin. Origin for question 3330: Joint-paper conjecture. Setup for question 3330: Let A⊂R² be finite, with no three collinear and no four cocircular, and 1≤k≤|A|−1. Write [I] for the centroid of I⊂A and A^(k)={[I]:|I|=k}. A level-k hypertriangulation is an edge-to-edge triangulation of conv A^(k) using some labeled points [I]∈A^(k), with |I∩J|=k−1 along every edge. Coincident centroids receive only one label. It is maximal if no other level-k hypertriangulation properly subdivides it.

**Source.** Herbert Edelsbrunner, Alexey Garber and Morteza Saghafian. *Order-2 Delaunay triangulations optimize angles*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aim.2024.110055) Location: Conjecture C, p. 30; Definition 2.2, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3330, checked 8 October 2026: Retained in the final paper; the later flips paper does not resolve it.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.mi.ras.ru/dis/ref09/garber.pdf)

## Q3350. Exact versus minimum degree

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 34

Is c′(r,t)=c(r,t) for all r≥3,t≥r−2?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3350, 3351, 3352: Asier Calbet Rípodas. Minimum Saturated Graphs. PhD, 2024, Queen Mary University of London. Advisors: Robert Johnson. Origin for questions 3350, 3351, 3352: Paper questions; the conical-vertex conjecture originates with Day. Setup for questions 3350, 3351, 3352: Graphs are finite and simple. H-saturated means H-subgraph-free but adding any missing edge creates H. For integers r≥3,t≥r−2, set c(r,t)=max{t|V(G)|−e(G): G is K_r-saturated, δ(G)=t}; define c′(r,t) using δ(G)≥t. Let sat(n,K_r,t) minimize e(G) over n-vertex K_r-saturated G with δ(G)≥t.

**Source.** Asier Calbet. *K_r-Saturated Graphs and the Two Families Theorem*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/12718) Location: Question 1, p. 7.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3350, 3351, 3352, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: final journal text retains these questions; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.13389) · [2](https://www.qmul.ac.uk/maths/postgraduate/postgraduate-research/our-phd-students-/our-phd-graduates/combinatorics-algebra-and-number-theory-phd-graduates/) · [3](https://qmro.qmul.ac.uk/xmlui/handle/123456789/101479)

## Q3353. Sharp twin-free saturation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

For every fixed r≥3, is tsat(n,K_r)=(r+3)n+o(n)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3353, 3354, 3355, 3356: see question 3350. Origin for questions 3353, 3354, 3355, 3356: Questions from Calbet’s paper, continuing his dissertation research. Setup for questions 3353, 3354, 3355, 3356: Graphs are finite and simple; H has an edge. H-saturated means H-subgraph-free but adding any missing edge creates H. Twins have identical open neighborhoods. Let tsat(n,H) be the minimum edges in a twin-free H-saturated n-vertex graph, when one exists; call H viable if such graphs exist for every sufficiently large n.

**Source.** Asier Calbet. *Twin-free K_r-saturated Graphs and Maximally Independent Sets in K_3-free Graphs*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19267v1) Location: After Theorem 2, p. 2.

## Q3357. Ramsey-sized chromatic bounds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.3) · **Collection** 34

For every χ-bounded hereditary C and integers m,s≥1, does C₀=C₀(C,m,s) exist such that every K_{s,…,s}-free G∈C satisfies χ(G)≤C₀R(s,ω(G)+1)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3357, 3358: Tung H. Nguyen. Induced Subgraph Density. PhD, 2025, Princeton University. Advisors: Paul Seymour. Origin for questions 3357, 3358: Joint paper conjecture and concluding question; not thesis-origin. Setup for questions 3357, 3358: Graphs are finite and simple; F-free means no induced F. A hereditary class C is χ-bounded if χ(G)≤f(ω(G)) for G∈C, for some f. Write K_{s,…,s} for the complete m-partite graph with parts of size s. R(s,w) is the least N forcing an independent s-set or a w-clique in every N-vertex graph.

**Source.** Tung H. Nguyen; Sang-il Oum. *Ramsey-type χ-bounds for χ-bounded graph classes*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08848v1) Location: Conjecture 1.3, p. 3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3357, 3358, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: current v1 retains both targets; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/nguyent/) · [2](https://www.pacm.princeton.edu/events/final-oral-public-examination-2) · [3](https://web.math.princeton.edu/~pds/papers/vita/vita.pdf)

## Q3359. Chromatic density for excluded trees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

For every finite tree T and every χ-bounded hereditary class C of T-free graphs, is C χ-dense?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3359, 3360: see question 3357. Origin for questions 3359, 3360: Paper questions, distinct from its delivered polynomial Gyárfás–Sumner conjecture. Setup for questions 3359, 3360: Graphs are finite and simple. T-free means induced-T-free; χ-bounded means χ≤f(ω) for some f. For ε>0, G is (ε,χ)-dense if χ(G[V(G)∖(N_G(v)∪{v})])<εχ(G) for every vertex v. A hereditary class C is χ-dense if each ε>0 admits δ>0 such that every G∈C has an induced (ε,χ)-dense F with χ(F)≥δχ(G).

**Source.** Tung H. Nguyen. *Polynomial χ-boundedness for excluding P₅*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24907v4) Location: §10, p. 42.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3359, 3360, checked 8 October 2026: 8 October: August v4 retains both questions; May companion also leaves density open.

## Q3361. Relative dimension of interval orders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 34

Is rdim uniformly bounded on interval orders, where x&lt;y means x’s interval lies strictly left of y’s?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3361, 3362, 3363: Jędrzej Hodor. Current doctoral student, Jagiellonian University. Advisors: Piotr Micek. Origin for questions 3361, 3362, 3363: Joint paper questions introducing relative dimension. Setup for questions 3361, 3362, 3363: For a finite nonempty poset P, a local realizer is a family L of linear extensions of induced subposets: each pair occurs together somewhere, and x≤\_P y iff every member containing both orders x≤y. Define rdim(P)=min_L Σ\_{M∈L}|M|/|P|; set rdim(∅)=0.

**Source.** Dominik Dürrschnabel; Jędrzej Hodor; Piotr Micek; Gerd Stumme; William T. Trotter. *Relative Dimension of Posets*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05166v1) Location: Question 1, p. 9.

## Q3365. Pathwidth inside optimal bags

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

For every proper minor-closed class C, does b=b(C) exist such that every G∈C admits an optimal tree-decomposition with pw(G[B])≤b for every bag B?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3365: see question 3361. Origin for question 3365: Joint paper’s concluding introductory question. Setup for question 3365: Graphs are finite and simple. An optimal tree-decomposition has width tw(G); the width is the largest bag size minus one. Bag pathwidth means pw(G[B]), using the induced subgraph on the bag. A proper minor-closed class omits some finite graph.

**Source.** Kevin Hendrey; Robert Hickingbotham; Jędrzej Hodor; David R. Wood. *Optimal Tree-Decompositions with Bags of Bounded Pathwidth*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27601v1) Location: Introduction, final paragraph, p. 4.

## Q3366. Sublinear cops in treedepth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 9) · **Collection** 34

For every ε>0, does D exist such that cop(G)≤εtd(G) whenever G is connected and td(G)≥D?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3366: see question 3361. Origin for question 3366: Joint paper problem. Setup for question 3366: On a finite connected simple graph, cops choose starting vertices before the visible robber. Each round all cops move, then the robber; each may stay or traverse one edge. Capture is sharing a vertex. cop(G) is the minimum winning number. Treedepth td(G) is the least vertex-height of a rooted tree whose ancestor-descendant closure contains G.

**Source.** Prosenjit Bose; Louis Esperet; Jędrzej Hodor; Gwenaël Joret; Piotr Micek; Clément Rambaud. *Cops and robber in graphs with bounded vertex cover number*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07435v1) Location: Problem 9, p. 10.

## Q3367. Coarse Gallai radius independent of packing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3) · **Collection** 34

Do f,g:N→N exist such that for all k,d≥1, every finite G and A⊆V(G) have either k A-paths pairwise at distance≥d, or X⊆V(G) with |X|≤f(k) such that B_G(X,g(d)) meets every A-path?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3367: see question 3361. Origin for question 3367: Authors’ Conjecture 3; Liu also attributes this form to Geelen, noting the lost oral wording. **Source.** Marc Distel; Ugo Giocanti; Jędrzej Hodor; Clément Legrand-Duchesne; Piotr Micek. *A Coarse Gallai Theorem*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18439v1) Location: Conjecture 3, p. 3.

## Q3368. Proper compression under minor exclusion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 34

Does f:N→N exist such that balls in every K_t-minor-free graph admit a proper compression scheme of size≤f(t)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3368, 3369, 3370: see question 3361. Origin for questions 3368, 3369, 3370: Joint paper questions; proper reconstruction is required. Setup for questions 3368, 3369, 3370: For a finite graph, balls have arbitrary integer radii, including empty balls. A proper compression scheme retains a labeled subsample and auxiliary bits from each realizable positive/negative sample; reconstruction returns a hyperedge agreeing with all original labels. Size is maximum retained vertices plus maximum bits.

**Source.** Romain Bourneuf; Jędrzej Hodor; Piotr Micek; Clément Rambaud. *Sample compression schemes for balls in structurally sparse graphs*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02949v1) Location: Question 4, p. 25.

<a id="q3421"></a>

## Q3421. Equal-density cospectral inapproximability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7) · **Collection** 35

Do cospectral U,W with ∫U=∫W exist for which no sequences of pairwise adjacency-cospectral finite graphs G_n,H_n converge respectively to U,W?

**Context.** Iľkovič: Leipzig doctoral defense 2026; advisors Daniel Kráľ and Richard Montgomery. Joint-paper question. Graphons are symmetric measurable [0,1]²→[0,1] functions, using Lebesgue measure. Cospectral means equal t(C_k,W)=∫∏\_{i=1}^k W(x_i,x_(i+1))dx_1⋯dx_k for every k≥3, with x_(k+1)=x_1. Cut distance is inf_φ sup_{S,T}|∫\_{S×T}(U(φ(x),φ(y))−W(x,y))dxdy|, over measure-preserving bijections φ and measurable S,T. Finite graphs are simple; convergence means cut-distance convergence of their equal-cell adjacency graphons, with orders tending to infinity.

**Source.** Jan Hladký; Daniel Iľkovič; Jared León; Xichao Shu. *On Cospectral Graphons*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/13768) Location: Problem 7, p. 9; §§2–3, pp. 4–6.

**Literature check.** Status: Final January 2026 text checked; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13229)


<a id="q3422"></a>

## Q3422. Scattered end-subspaces inside the same graph

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.12) · **Collection** 35

For every graph G and scattered X⊆Ω(G), is X homeomorphic to Ω(H) for some induced H⊆G? If not, does a not-necessarily-induced H⊆G always suffice?

**Context.** Magalhães Júnior: São Paulo PhD 2025; advisor Leandro Fiorini Aurichi. Joint-paper questions. An order tree is rooted with well-ordered predecessor sets. R(T) consists of nonempty downward-closed chains without maxima, as a subspace of {0,1}^T. Ray spaces are homeomorphic to such R(T). Ω(G) consists of graph rays equivalent when no finite vertex set separates their tails, with basic neighborhoods from components after finite vertex deletion. Scattered means each nonempty subspace has an isolated point. First-countable means each point has a countable neighborhood base.

**Source.** Matheus Duzi; Gabriel Fernandes; Paulo Magalhães Júnior. *Ray and end spaces: characterizations and classification up to homeomorphism*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11561v2) Location: Question 5.12, p. 37.

**Literature check.** Status: July v2 retains both questions; no later resolution located.

Scattered means each nonempty subspace has an isolated point. First-countable means each point has a countable neighborhood base.

## Q3424. A disjoint double-ray cover

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.10) · **Collection** 35

Must G contain a family of pairwise vertex-disjoint double rays covering A?

**Context.** Magalhães Júnior: São Paulo PhD 2025; advisor Leandro Fiorini Aurichi. Thesis question extending Salia’s double-Hall program. G=(A,B) is a connected infinite simple bipartite graph with A,B infinite. Put N²(X)={b∈B:|N(b)∩X|≥2}; assume |N²(X)|≥|X| for every X⊆A with |X|≥2. Each a∈A has finite degree; each b∈B has either finite or cofinite neighborhood in A. A double ray is a two-way infinite simple path.

**Source.** Paulo Sérgio Farias Magalhães Júnior. *Relationships between topology and combinatorics*. University of São Paulo, 2025. Advisor(s): Leandro Fiorini Aurichi. [primary source](https://teses.usp.br/teses/disponiveis/55/55135/tde-01092025-112137/publico/PauloSergioFariasMagalhaesJunior_DO_revisada.pdf) Location: Question 9.10, p. 133; §4.1, pp. 61–69.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02816)

## Q3425. Three-dimensional cube densities

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 35

Is π\_k(3)=3/4 for every k∈{0,1,2}?

**Context.** Baber: UCL PhD 2011; advisor John Talbot. Baber–Talbot conjecture in §1. A k-face of {0,1}^n fixes n−k coordinates and leaves k free. Let ex_k(n,d) be the largest number of k-faces in a family that contains no full collection of k-faces of any d-face. Set π\_k(d)=lim_{n→∞}ex_k(n,d)/(2^(n−k)binom(n,k)).

**Source.** Rahil Baber; Natalie Behague; Asier Calbet; David Ellis; Joshua Erde; Ron Gray; Maria-Romina Ivan; Barnabás Janzer; Robert Johnson; Luka Milićević; John Talbot; Ta Sheng Tan; Belinda Wickes. *A collection of open problems in celebration of Imre Leader’s 60th birthday*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.18163v1) Location: Conjecture 1.1, p. 3.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09445)

## Q3426. Long cycles in every matching pair

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.5) · **Collection** 35

**Context.** Behague: QMUL PhD 2020; supervisors Robert Johnson and Mark Walters. Thesis questions continuing the 2019 factorization paper. For integer d≥2, Q_d has vertices {0,1}^d and edges between strings differing in one coordinate. A 1-factorization partitions its edges into d perfect matchings M_1,…,M_d. ## Q3428. Exact connectivity threshold for cube factors

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.7) · **Collection** 35

Determine r(d) for every integer d≥3.

**Context.** Hollom: Cambridge PhD completed January 2026; 2025 thesis; advisor Béla Bollobás. Behague originated r(d); random-model conjecture belongs to this joint paper. Q_d is the graph on {0,1}^d joining strings differing in one coordinate. A 1-factorization M partitions all edges into d perfect matchings. Let r(M) be the smallest r∈{1,…,d} for which every union of r distinct members is connected, and r(d)=min_M r(M).

**Source.** Lawrence Hollom; Benedict Randall Shaw. *Connecting hypercube 1-factors*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15698v1) Location: Introduction, p. 1; §3, pp. 12–13; Behague thesis Question 3.7, p. 82.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: 3≤r(d)≤45log₂d for d≥3000. The subcase r(6)=3 is settled; the general targets remain unresolved in inspected sources.

## Q3479. Walker’s spanning cycle square

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.1) · **Collection** 35

For every fixed ε∈(0,1) and p=p(n)∈[n^(−1/2+ε),1], does Walker have, with probability tending to one, a strategy on G(n,p) to claim a Hamilton cycle together with all edges joining vertices at cyclic distance two?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Mogge, above. Origin: Joint-paper Problem 5.1, repeated as thesis Problem 4.3.1. Setup: Walker selects her initial vertex. Each round, moving first, she makes two successive edge-steps, claiming traversed unclaimed edges; she may reuse her edges but never traverse Breaker’s. Breaker then claims two unused edges. G(n,p) has independent edge probability p.

**Source.** Dennis Clemens, Pranshu Gupta, Yannick Mogge. *Walker-Breaker Games on G_{n,p}*. 2024. [primary source](https://tore.tuhh.de/bitstreams/c3700acf-4f1d-4b82-b779-1333e6e99a09/download) Location: Problem 5.1, p. 21.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Final paper and 2025 thesis retain the problem; arXiv ends at v2. Subsequent and erratum searches found no resolution.


<a id="q3480"></a>

## Q3480. Two-pass hare-sortable Cayley words

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 35

Determine, for each integer n≥1, the number of length-n Cayley words w for which h(h(w)) is weakly increasing.

**Context.** Origin: Paper Open Problem 1, repeated as thesis Open Problem 8.1. Setup: A Cayley word uses each integer from 1 through its maximum. To apply h, read left-to-right, pushing the next letter onto a stack whenever this keeps stack entries weakly increasing from top to bottom; otherwise pop to output until pushing is legal. Flush after input ends.

**Source.** Giulio Cerbai. *Sorting Cayley permutations with pattern-avoiding machines*. 2021. [primary source](https://ajc.maths.uq.edu.au/pdf/80/ajc_v80_p322.pdf) Location: Open Problem 1, p. 330.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current primary retains the question. A 2026 follow-up solves hare pop-stack enumeration, a different operation; targeted searches found no double-hare enumeration.


**Result (2026-10-09).** A finite automaton gives exact counts for each fixed alphabet and a matrix/inclusion-exclusion formula for every length. For Cayley words using exactly four symbols, the generating function is z^4(22-119z+162z^2)/((1-z)(1-2z)(1-3z)^4), with asymptotic n^3*3^n/324. The explicit rational closed formula concerns the four-symbol slice. A closed generating function and useful uniform asymptotics for the unrestricted sum over alphabet sizes remain unresolved; the exponential-size general automaton is not presented as a complete resolution.

[Proof](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/discrete/Q3480-proof.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/algebra/review-Q3480.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/discrete/q3480_certificate.json) · [Source check](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/discrete/SOURCES.md). This is a research result with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed or formally certified theorem.


<a id="q3481"></a>

## Q3481. Counting machine Wilf classes

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.3) · **Collection** 35

Determine, for every k≥2, the number of PAM-Wilf-equivalence classes of patterns σ∈S_k.

**Context.** Origin: Thesis Open Problem 3.3. Setup: For a pattern σ, s_σ reads a permutation through a stack: push the next entry whenever the top-to-bottom stack avoids σ, otherwise pop to output until legal; flush after input. Avoidance means no subsequence has relative order σ. Let f_σ(n) count π∈S_n for which s_{21}(s_σ(π))=12…n. Two patterns are PAM-Wilf-equivalent if their f-sequences agree for every n.

**Source.** Giulio Cerbai. *Sorting permutations with pattern-avoiding machines*. University of Florence, 2021. Advisor(s): Luca Ferrari, verified on thesis cover and acknowledgements. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.03621v1) Location: Open Problem 3.3, p. 30.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current thesis version and author’s publication list inspected; targeted later-result and correction searches found no classification.


<a id="q3483"></a>

## Q3483. Ten clean vertices with two cleaners

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 18) · **Collection** 35

For every connected G, can two radius-one cleaners make at least min{10,|V(G)|} vertices clean simultaneously?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Mogge, above. Origin: Joint-paper questions; not thesis-origin claims. Setup: In a finite simple graph, place k cleaners; gas initially fills vertices farther than ℓ from them. Each turn each cleaner stays or moves one edge, clearing its closed radius-ℓ neighbourhood. Gas then spreads simultaneously one edge except into these neighbourhoods. Count clean vertices immediately after cleaning. Let c_ℓ and i_ℓ be the least k allowing respectively zero or at most one gaseous vertex at some such time.

**Source.** Bojan Bašić, Alfie Davies, Aleksa Džuklevski, Strahinja Gvozdić, Yannick Mogge. *Seeing is not believing in limited visibility cops and robbers*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00941v1) Location: Conjecture 18, p. 22.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current v1 retains both questions; targeted subsequent and correction searches located no resolution.

## Q3486. Uniform bound for reliability roots

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

Does an absolute constant C bound |z| for every complex zero z of every R_G?

**Context.** Mol: On Connectedness and Graph Polynomials, Dalhousie PhD 2016; supervisor Jason Brown. Origin: Doctoral-research questions; global boundedness predates the thesis. Setup: For a finite connected loopless multigraph G=(V,E), set R_G(q)=Σ\_A(1−q)^|A|q^(|E|−|A|), summing over edge sets A for which (V,A) is connected. This is the failure-probability convention. Simple graphs have no parallel edges.

**Source.** Jason Brown and Lucas Mol. *On the roots of all-terminal reliability polynomials*. 2017. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1703.10566) Location: §4, p. 22.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: The exact boundedness question is reaffirmed by Buys (2026), Introduction, and Bencs–Piombi–Regts, §6. The 2017 publisher text was not inspected.

<a id="q3487"></a>

## Q3487. Triangle score with degree four

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 35

Determine the sharp asymptotic value of g(n) when Constructor’s graph must have maximum degree at most four throughout play.

**Context.** Doctoral context: Mogge, above. Origin: Joint-paper questions in concluding remarks, not thesis-origin. Setup: On E(K_n), Constructor moves first; players alternately take one unused edge. Constructor obeys the specified restriction, Blocker has none. Play ends when Constructor has no legal edge. Constructor maximizes and Blocker minimizes the final number of Constructor’s triangles; their optimal value is g(n).

**Source.** Chloé Boisson, Yannick Mogge, Aline Parreau, Théo Pierron. *Creating triangles in Constructor-Blocker games*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05811v1) Location: §5, p. 39 (g(n, K₃, S₅)).

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Current v1 retains both gaps; subsequent and correction searches found no solution.

<a id="q3491"></a>

## Q3491. Finite geodetic Cayley classification

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6) · **Collection** 35

Must every finite geodetic Cayley graph be a complete graph or an odd cycle?

**Context.** Origin: Q3491 is Federici’s conjecture. Q3492 is Benjamini’s question, retained in joint doctoral work with Carmesin and Georgakopoulos. Setup: For a finite group G and inverse-closed generating S⊆G∖{1}, Cay(G,S) has edges {g,gs}. Geodetic means every vertex pair has exactly one shortest path. Sphere-packable in ℝ³ means representable by positive-radius, interior-disjoint balls, with tangency exactly encoding adjacency. Transience refers to simple random walk. Sphere-packing definition: https://arxiv.org/pdf/0907.2609v4, Definition 2.1, pp. 2–3.

**Source.** Bruno Federici. *Interactions between large-scale invariants in infinite graphs*. University of Warwick, 2017. Advisor(s): Agelos Georgakopoulos. [primary source](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/108882/1/WRAP_Theses_Federici_2017.pdf) Location: Conjecture 6, p. 55.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: corrected geodetic paper verifies group orders≤1024; subsequent infinite-group constructions do not settle Q3491. Bounded searches found no resolution of either question.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/0907.2609v4) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.00261v4)

## Q3498. Crown real-rootedness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.18) · **Collection** 35

Does h\*\_{C₂ₙ}(t) have only real roots for every n≥2?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Teemu Lundström, Aalto University doctoral thesis 2026; advisors Kaie Kubjas (supervising professor), Ragnar Freij-Hollanti (advisor). Origin: Doctoral joint paper; thesis occurrence unverified. Authors’ conjectures and question. Setup: For finite poset P, let O(P)={x∈[0,1]^P:xₚ≤xᵣ whenever p≤r}, E_P(m)=|mO(P)∩ℤ^P|, and h\*\_P(t)=(1−t)^{|P|+1}∑\_{m≥0}E_P(m)tᵐ. The crown C₂ₙ has elements aᵢ,bᵢ and covers aᵢ&lt;bᵢ, aᵢ₊₁&lt;bᵢ, with i modulo n. Let L(Pₙ) be the inclusion-ordered faces of a convex n-gon, including ∅ and Pₙ; Bₙ is all subsets of {1,…,n}, ordered by inclusion.

**Source.** Teemu Lundström, Leonardo Saud Maia Leite. *Order polytopes of crown posets*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.05123v5) Location: Conjecture 5.18, p. 30.

<a id="q3499"></a>

## Q3499. Polygon-face Ehrhart positivity

**Status:** Solved here: disproved · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.5) · **Collection** 35

Does E_{L(Pₙ)}(m), expanded in powers of m, have nonnegative coefficients for every n≥3?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Teemu Lundström, Aalto University doctoral thesis 2026; advisors Kaie Kubjas (supervising professor), Ragnar Freij-Hollanti (advisor). Origin: Doctoral joint paper; thesis occurrence unverified. Authors’ conjectures and question. Setup: For finite poset P, let O(P)={x∈[0,1]^P:xₚ≤xᵣ whenever p≤r}, E_P(m)=|mO(P)∩ℤ^P|, and h\*\_P(t)=(1−t)^{|P|+1}∑\_{m≥0}E_P(m)tᵐ. The crown C₂ₙ has elements aᵢ,bᵢ and covers aᵢ&lt;bᵢ, aᵢ₊₁&lt;bᵢ, with i modulo n. Let L(Pₙ) be the inclusion-ordered faces of a convex n-gon, including ∅ and Pₙ; Bₙ is all subsets of {1,…,n}, ordered by inclusion.

**Source.** Teemu Lundström, Leonardo Saud Maia Leite. *Order polytopes of crown posets*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.05123v5) Location: Question 6.5, p. 33.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: latest v5 (October 1) retains all three. New face-count and circular-fence positivity results do not resolve them.

**Literature check.** Status sources: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.23629v1; https://doi.org/10.1112/plms.70204.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.23629v1) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1112/plms.70204)


**Result (2026-10-09).** The full face lattice of a convex 13-gon, including the empty and full faces, has Ehrhart linear coefficient -298495711/35302608. This is the only negative coefficient of its degree-28 Ehrhart polynomial; every coefficient is positive for polygon sizes 3 through 12. This disproves the Ehrhart coefficient question, distinct from the source's earlier negative order-polynomial coefficient for a 7-gon. Two separate exact arithmetic derivations verify the counterexample; no claim about other poset families is made.

[Proof](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/discrete/Q3499-proof.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/algebra/review-Q3499.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/discrete/q3499_certificate.json) · [Source check](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/discrete/SOURCES.md). This is a research result with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed or formally certified theorem.

## Q3569. Periodic zero-extension

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 9.1) · **Collection** 36

For p≥1, is [w∈L_U iff w0^p∈L_U for every w∈A\*] equivalent to having infinite words a_0,…,a_{p−1} with rep_U(U_{np+i}−1)=Pref_{np+i}(a_i) for all n≥0 and 0≤i&lt;p, necessarily satisfying σ^j(a_i)≤lex a_{i−j mod p} for every j≥0? Here σ deletes the first letter.

**Context.** Degree: Docteur en Sciences Advisors: Émilie Charlier. Source type: Doctoral dissertation Origin: Thesis conjecture; Rigo’s context-free question. Setup: Let U=(U_n) be strictly increasing positive integers, U_0=1, with bounded successive ratios. Write rep_U for greedy representation over alphabet A, L_U=0\*rep_U(N), and Max_U={rep_U(U_n−1):n≥0}; Pref_k means length-k prefix.

**Source.** Savinien Kreczman. *Linear numeration systems without a dominant root, alternate base numeration systems, and their links*. Université de Liège, 2026. Advisor(s): Émilie Charlier. [primary source](https://orbi.uliege.be/handle/2268/345086) Location: Conjecture 9.1, printed p.280; definitions pp.12–13.

**Literature check.** Status: No resolution located by 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://orbi.uliege.be/bitstream/2268/345086/1/Kreczman_PhD_Thesis_JuryRemarks.pdf)

**Result (2026-10-09).** For every p>=1, validity is preserved and reflected by appending p zeroes exactly when maximal greedy words are nested along residue classes modulo p. Their unique infinite limits satisfy every stated cross-residue lexicographic shift inequality. Full Conjecture 9.1 for positional U-systems; the separate alternate-base and noncanonical-shift conjectures are not resolved.

[Proof](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/discrete/Q3569-periodic-zero-extension.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/algebra/review-Q3569.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/discrete/periodic-zero-checks.json). This is a research proposal with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed result.

Setup: For finite Z⊂Q^n, let J_Z be generated by highest-degree parts of polynomials vanishing on Z, and V_Z={h∈Q[x_1,…,x_n]:f(∂)h=0 for all f∈J_Z}. Set H_P=⊕\_{r≥0}x_0^r V_{rP∩Z^n}, with ordinary multiplication. Let P_{ℓ,m,n}={x∈[0,m]^n:Σx_i=ℓ}.

**Source.** Nathaniel Libman, Weston Miller. *Graded Ehrhart theory for hypersimplices*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27438) Location: §6, third bullet, printed p.19.

**Literature check.** Status: Inspected arXiv:2608.27438v1; bounded 2026-10-09 check found no later resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://mathweb.ucsd.edu/~bprhoades/advising.html)


<a id="q3576"></a>

## Q3576. Proximity under order, size and vertex-connectivity constraints

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

For admissible n,m,κ, determine the maximum π(G) among κ-connected G.

**Context.** Degree: Philosophiae Doctor in Mathematics Advisors: Peter Dankelmann, Sonwabile Mafunda. Source type: Doctoral dissertation Origin: Formalizations of the dissertation’s unnumbered future directions. Setup: For a connected simple n-vertex graph G, n≥2, with at least m edges, set a_G(v)=(n−1)^{-1}Σ\_u d_G(u,v), remoteness ρ(G)=max_v a_G(v), and proximity π(G)=min_v a_G(v). Connectivity constraints are lower bounds.

**Source.** Sufiyan Arif Mallu. *Bounds on proximity and remoteness in graphs and digraphs*. University of Johannesburg, 2025. Advisor(s): Peter Dankelmann, Sonwabile Mafunda. [primary source](https://ujcontent.uj.ac.za/esploro/outputs/doctoral/Bounds-on-proximity-and-remoteness-in/9960708807691) Location: Conclusion, p.136; proximity definition Chapter 1.

**Literature check.** Status: Later digraph/outerplanar results do not resolve these cases; checked 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.uj.ac.za/members/mr-sufiyani-mallu/)


<a id="q3577"></a>

## Q3577. Contiguity of the random regular graph process

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 36

As n→∞ through nd even, are P_n and U_n mutually contiguous, meaning P_n(A_n)→0 iff U_n(A_n)→0 for every sequence of graph events A_n?

**Context.** Degree: PhD in Mathematics Advisors: Lutz Warnke. Source type: Doctoral dissertation restating an earlier conjecture Origin: Wormald’s 1999 Conjecture 6.3, restated in the thesis and Molloy–Surya–Warnke paper. Setup: Fix d≥2. On labeled vertices [n], repeatedly add a uniformly chosen missing edge whose endpoints both have degree below d, stopping when none is available. Let P_n be the final graph’s law conditioned on d-regularity, and U_n the uniform simple d-regular graph law.

**Source.** Erlang Surya. *Concentration and Sharp Thresholds in Random Graphs*. University of California San Diego, 2025. Advisor(s): Lutz Warnke. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/7vt625fh) Location: Thesis Chapter 2, pp.4–5; final JCTB 176 (2026), Introduction.

**Literature check.** Status: January 2026 journal introduction explicitly retains it; irregular-degree noncontiguity theorem excludes this case. Bounded check 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://mathweb.ucsd.edu/~lwarnke/PhD_Thesis_ErlangSurya.pdf) · [2](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0095895625000577)


<a id="q3578"></a>

## Q3578. Polynomial clique-degeneracy bounds forbidding broadleaved stars

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6) · **Collection** 36

For every r≥3, ζ≥2 and r-broadleaved ζ-star H, is there k>0 such that degen_r(G)≤b(G)^k for every graph G with no induced H?

**Context.** Degree: PhD in Combinatorics and Optimization Advisors: Sophie Spirkl, Rowan Yeats. Source type: Doctoral dissertation Origin: Problem in ongoing joint work with LaGrange, Rundström and Spirkl, incorporated in the dissertation. Setup: An r-broadleaved ζ-star replaces each leaf of K_{1,ζ} by a nonempty clique of size at most r−1, preserving adjacency to the center. Let d_r^F(v) count a largest vertex-disjoint family of (r−1)-cliques in N_F(v), degen_r(G)=max_{∅≠F⊆G} min_v d_r^F(v), and b(G)=max{t:K_{t,t}⊆G}, with b=0 for edgeless graphs.

**Source.** Aristotelis Chaniotis. *Preserving and Generalizing χ-boundedness*. University of Waterloo, 2025. Advisor(s): Sophie Spirkl, Rowan Yeats. [primary source](https://uwspace.uwaterloo.ca/items/26df24d4-8ee7-4bab-9975-be038b27d919) Location: Problem 6, printed p.118; definitions pp.33–35.

**Literature check.** Status: Source gives only an unspecified bounding function. No polynomial-bound resolution located by 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://uwspace.uwaterloo.ca/bitstreams/9a05e803-e0ed-4ee6-a495-2ff062ffb57b/download)

## Q3579. A general combinatorial rule for complementary-sum Chern plethysm

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 26) · **Collection** 36

Give a cancellation-free combinatorial interpretation of c_{λμ}(n) for arbitrary λ,μ,n in the stated range.

**Context.** Degree: PhD defence verified; award unverified. Advisors: Brendon Rhoades. Source type: Earlier joint paper; doctoral genealogy verified Paper: Combinatorics and Representation Theory of Special Cases of Chern Plethysm Authors: Nathaniel Libman, Gidon Orelowitz Origin: Joint-paper open problem, not verified as thesis-originated. Setup: Let s_λ be the Schur polynomial in n variables. For partitions λ,μ with length at most n and |λ|=|μ|, define c_{λμ}(n)=[s_μ]s_λ(X−x_1,…,X−x_n), where X=x_1+⋯+x_n.

**Source.** Nathaniel Libman, Gidon Orelowitz. *Combinatorics and Representation Theory of Special Cases of Chern Plethysm*. 2024. [primary source](https://www.combinatorics.org/ojs/index.php/eljc/article/view/v31i2p20) Location: Final §7, Problem 26, p.20; definitions pp.4–5. Preprint Problem 1 is renumbered.

**Literature check.** Status: Final April 2024 article inspected; bounded 2026-10-09 author/topic checks found no general combinatorial rule.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.combinatorics.org/ojs/index.php/eljc/article/download/v31i2p20/pdf)

## Q3580. Rigid-graph realizations in even norms

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.1) · **Collection** 36

For even p≥4 and d≥1, if G is rigid in ℓ\_p^d, is |D_G(P)|≥c_{G,p,d}|P|^{|V|−1} for every nonempty finite P⊂R^d, for some c_{G,p,d}>0?

**Context.** Degree: PhD, awarded 13 May 2025 Advisors: Misha Rudnev. Source type: Joint paper after dissertation-first access attempt Paper: Generalised Erdős distance theory on graphs Authors: Sean Dewar, Nora Frankl, Samuel Mansfield, Anthony Nixon, Jonathan Passant, Audie Warren Origin: Authors’ May 2025 paper conjectures. Setup: Let D_G(P)={(‖p_u−p_v‖)\_{uv∈E}:p∈P^V} for G=(V,E). Generic ℓ\_p-rigidity means generic vertex placements have finitely many realizations with their edge lengths, modulo isometries.

**Source.** Sean Dewar, Nora Frankl, Samuel Mansfield, Anthony Nixon, Jonathan Passant, Audie Warren. *Generalised Erdős distance theory on graphs*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06590) Location: Conjecture 6.1, p.39; metric and rigidity definitions §§1.2–1.3.

**Literature check.** Status: Only v1; no resolution located by 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://research-information.bris.ac.uk/en/studentTheses/geometric-and-arithmetic-structure-in-finite-point-sets/)

Keranen Origin: Unresolved case identified in the dissertation and subsequent joint paper. Setup: A C_k-factor is a spanning disjoint union of k-cycles. ## Q3583. Bounded-depth minor extensions of arbitrary matroids

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 36

Is there a function f such that every finite nonempty matroid M is a minor of some matroid N with cdd(N)≤f(bd(M))?

**Context.** Degree: PhD defence verified; award unverified. Advisors: Daniel Král. Source type: Joint paper; dissertation access attempted; advisor verifies May 2025 PhD defence Paper: Branch-depth is minor closure of contraction-deletion-depth Authors: Marcin Briański, Daniel Král, Kristýna Pekárková Origin: Joint-paper conjecture. Setup: For finite nonempty matroid M with ground set E, λ(X)=r(X)+r(E−X)−r(E). For |E|≥2, bd(M) minimizes k over trees with an internal vertex and leaves bijective to E, radius≤k, and λ(X)≤k for unions X of components after deleting an internal vertex; bd(singleton)=0. Define cdd(singleton)=1; for disconnected M take the maximum cdd of its components; otherwise cdd(M)=1+min_e min(cdd(M/e),cdd(M\e)).

**Source.** Marcin Briański, Daniel Král, Kristýna Pekárková. *Branch-depth is minor closure of contraction-deletion-depth*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16215) Location: Conjecture 1, p.17; Conjecture 2 is equivalent and not counted separately.

**Literature check.** Status: Reaffirmed in Measuring Depth of Matroids v3, p.36 (2026-10-02).

**Further links.** [1](https://www.ucw.cz/~kral/cv-stud.html)

## Q3620. Parallel-hyperplane mass bisection

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.4) · **Collection** 37

For all integers d,k≥1, can any d+k−1 absolutely continuous probability measures on R^d be simultaneously bisected by the chessboard coloring of at most k parallel hyperplanes?

**Context.** Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. Origin: Older Soberón–Takahashi and Tverberg–Vrećica conjectures, restated in the thesis. Setup: For parallel affine hyperplanes, their chessboard coloring alternates black and white across consecutive slabs. A bisection gives each color half the measure. All partitions below have nonempty parts.

**Source.** Nikola Sadovek. *Obstructions across geometry and topology*. Freie Universität Berlin, 2025. Advisor(s): Pavle Blagojević. [primary source](https://refubium.fu-berlin.de/bitstream/handle/fub188/51916/Sadovek%20-%20Doctoral%20dissertation.pdf?isAllowed=y&sequence=1) Location: Conjecture 2.4, printed p.18 (PDF p.30).

**Literature check.** Status: Open in thesis; bisection is reaffirmed in June 2026 v3, Conjecture 1.5. No general resolution located, 2026-10-09.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2404.14320v3) · [2](https://refubium.fu-berlin.de/handle/fub188/51916) · [3](https://www.mi.fu-berlin.de/fb/dates/disputationen/Sadovek_-Nikola---MI---Aushang.pdf)

## Q3669. Double-star exclusions and domatic growth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3) · **Collection** 37

For every fixed s,t≥1, is the class with no induced S_{s,t} DOM-bounded?

**Context.** Doctoral connection: Variations on the graph colouring problem; Université Paris-Saclay, 2026; advisers Nathalie Aubrun, François Pirot. Graphs are finite, nonempty and simple; δ is minimum degree. DOM(G) maximizes parts in a vertex partition each meeting every closed neighbourhood. DOM-bounded means DOM(G)≥f(δ(G)) for some unbounded f:Z_{≥0}→R_{>0}. S_{s,t} is an edge with s,t pendant leaves at its ends. Authors: Quentin Chuet, Selma Djelloul, Hoang La, François Pirot, Hossein Zaredehabadi. Remaining double-star conjecture; explicit problems.

**Source.** Quentin Chuet, Selma Djelloul, Hoang La, François Pirot and Hossein Zaredehabadi. *A Domatic Analogue of χ-Bounded Graph Classes and the Gyárfás–Sumner Conjecture*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02030) Location: Conjecture 3, p.2; stars and P4 settled.

**Literature check.** Status: v1, June 2026; no resolution found by 2026-10-09.

<a id="q3671"></a>

## Q3671. Fractional domatic spectrum

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 37

Determine {FDOM(G):G is a nonempty finite simple graph}.

**Context.** Doctoral connection: Variations on the graph colouring problem; Université Paris-Saclay, 2026; advisers Nathalie Aubrun, François Pirot. For nonempty finite simple G, δ is minimum degree and girth is shortest-cycle length. A dominating (p:q)-colouring assigns each vertex q colours from [p]={1,…,p}, with all p colours on each closed neighbourhood; p,q are positive integers. FDOM(G)=sup p/q over these colourings. Put F(g)=inf{FDOM(G):δ(G)=2,girth(G)≥g}; F_P(g) restricts this infimum to planar G. Authors: Quentin Chuet, Hugo Demaret, Hoang La, François Pirot. Explicit problems and conjecture.

**Source.** Quentin Chuet, Hugo Demaret, Hoang La and François Pirot. *Fractional domatic number and minimum degree*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19617) Location: Problem 3, p.31.

**Literature check.** Status: v1, 2025-08-27; no resolution found by 2026-10-09.

For nonempty finite simple G, δ is minimum degree and girth is shortest-cycle length. <a id="q3673"></a>

## Q3673. Forbidden subgraphs reducing frugal colouring growth

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem A) · **Collection** 37

For fixed β≥1, classify the bipartite graphs H satisfying χβ(Δ,H)=o(Δ^{1+1/β}) as Δ→∞.

**Context.** Doctoral connection: Variations on the graph colouring problem; Université Paris-Saclay, 2026; advisers Nathalie Aubrun, François Pirot. Graphs are finite and simple; β≥1 is integral. A β-frugal colouring is proper, with each colour used at most β times in each open neighbourhood. Let χβ(Δ,H) maximize the minimum such colour count over H-subgraph-free graphs of maximum degree at most Δ. Let χ\_{2,H}(Δ) maximize the minimum proper-colouring count avoiding bichromatic H, over all graphs of maximum degree at most Δ. Authors: Quentin Chuet. v3, 2026-10-04; Problem C has been solved.

**Source.** Quentin Chuet. *Frugal colourings of graphs via sparse hypergraph colouring*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23379) Location: Problem A, p.3.

**Literature check.** Status: Problems A–B; subsequently reaffirmed in arXiv:2609.39716.
